In fact, one can read off the bound for \(\| F^{\varepsilon} \|_{C, \alpha, \beta, \kappa}\) directly from (4.21) (which holds for any \(k \geqslant 0\)), provided that \(C\) and \(\lambda\) are small enough, the latter depending on \(C\). Finally, comparing with (4.11) we have \(H_{> n, s}^{\varepsilon [\ell] (k)} = \boldsymbol{1}_{n < \ell \leqslant 2 n} \sum_{\ell' = 0}^{\ell} \sum_{k' = 0}^{k - 1} \mathbb{C}_{\dot{G}_s^{\varepsilon}} (F_s^{\varepsilon [\ell'] (k - k')}, F_s^{\varepsilon [\ell - \ell'] (k' + 1)})\), and therefore, by the proof of Theorem 4.13 we have the bound \(\| H_{> n ; s}^{\varepsilon [\ell] (k)} \|_{\mathcal{L}^{(k)}_t (s ; c_{\ell})} \lesssim k (\ell + 1) C^{k + 1} 2^{(\alpha - \beta k) s} 2^{- (d + \beta - \alpha - 2 \gamma) s} 2^{- \kappa \ell s} {\| F \|^2}^{}_{C, \alpha, \beta, \kappa}\), which implies the claim.\(\Box\)

Remark 4.16. We first of all note that because of the symmetries in the internal component, one can show that for any \(\mu, \mu', \bar{\mu} \in \{ \uparrow, \downarrow \} \times \{ \pm \}\)

\(\displaystyle F^{\varepsilon [\ell] (1)}_t ((y ; \mu) ; (x ; \mu')) = \delta_{\mu, \mu'} F^{\varepsilon [\ell] (1)}_t ((y ; \bar{\mu}) ; (x ; \bar{\mu})),\)

that is, \((F^{\varepsilon [\ell] (1)}_t ((y ; \mu) ; (x ; \mu')))_{\mu, \mu'}\) is diagonal and constant.

Given the global solution, and taking Remark 4.16 and eq. (3.30) into account, we can finally define the renormalised chemical potential.

Definition 4.17. The scale-dependent chemical potential \(\mu^{\varepsilon}_t\) associated with the global solution \(F^{\varepsilon}\) of the truncated flow equations is defined as, for any \(\mu \in \{ \uparrow, \downarrow \} \times \{ \pm \}\)

\(\displaystyle \mu^{\varepsilon}_t := -\operatorname{sign} (\mu) \sum_{\ell = 0}^n \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} (F^{\varepsilon [\ell] (1)}_t ((0 ; \mu) ; (x ; \mu)))_0 \mathrm{d} x,\)

where \(F^{\varepsilon [\ell] (1)}_t = ((F^{\varepsilon [\ell] (1)}_t)_0, (F^{\varepsilon [\ell] (1)}_t)_2)\), \((F^{\varepsilon [\ell] (1)}_t)_0\) being the local part, and \(\operatorname{sign} (\mu)\) is defined in (3.28). The renormalised chemical potential is \(\mu_{\infty}^{\varepsilon} := \lim_{T \rightarrow \infty} \mu_T^{\varepsilon}\).

Remark 4.18. Note the bound

\(\displaystyle | \mu^{\varepsilon}_T | \leqslant \sum_{\ell = 0}^n \| \operatorname{Loc}F^{\varepsilon [\ell] (1)}_T \|_{\mathcal{L} \mathfrak{^{}}^{(1)}_T} \lesssim_n \| F^{\varepsilon} \|_{C, \alpha, \beta, \kappa} 2^{(\alpha - \beta - \kappa) T}\)

where by (4.14) and the second constraint in (4.15) \(\alpha - \beta - \kappa > 2 \gamma\).

Next, we prove the convergence \(F^{\varepsilon} \rightarrow F\) in a weaker topology.

Proposition 4.19. (Continuum limit). Let \(\varepsilon > \varepsilon' \in 2^{-\mathbb{N}_{\infty}}\). Under the same assumptions of Theorem 4.13 but with \(\alpha > 3 \beta\), let \(F^{\varepsilon}\) and \(F^{\varepsilon'}\) be the global solutions of the truncated flow equation and let \(\theta \in \left( 0, \frac{\alpha - 3 \beta}{2} \right]\). Then

\(\displaystyle \| F^{\varepsilon} - F^{\varepsilon'} \|_{C, \alpha, \beta, \kappa} \lesssim_{n, \theta} \varepsilon^{\theta} \sup_{\#= \varepsilon, \varepsilon'} (\| F^{\#} \|_{C, \alpha - \theta, \beta, \kappa} + \| F^{\#} \|_{C, \alpha - \theta, \beta, \kappa}^{n + 2}) .\)

Proof. Set

\(\displaystyle \delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} F_s := F_s^{\varepsilon} - F_s^{\varepsilon'}, \hspace{3em} \delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} \dot{G}_s := \dot{G}_s^{\varepsilon} - \dot{G}_s^{\varepsilon'} . \) (4.25)

We can write the truncated flow equation for the difference as follows:

\(\displaystyle \partial_s \delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} F_s^{[\ell + 1] (k)} = \delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} \mathbb{D}^2_{\dot{G}_s} (F_s^{[\ell] (k + 2)}) + \sum_{\ell' = 0}^{\ell} \sum_{k' = 1}^k \delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} \mathbb{C}_{\dot{G}_s} (F_s^{[\ell'] (k')}, F_s^{[\ell - \ell'] (k + 1 - k')}) \) (4.26)

with identically null boundary data \({\delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'}}_{} F_0^{[0]} = 0\). Above, the difference operator \(\delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'}\) acts on \(\dot{G}_s\) as well,

\(\displaystyle \delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} \mathbb{D}^2_{\dot{G}_s} (F_s^{[\ell] (k)}) =\mathbb{D}^2_{\dot{G}_s^{\varepsilon}} (\delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} F_s^{[\ell] (k)}) +\mathbb{D}^2_{\delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} \dot{G}_s} (F_s^{\varepsilon' [\ell] (k)})\)

and similar identity holds for \(\delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} \mathbb{C}_{\dot{G}_s}\)

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{l} \delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} \mathbb{C}_{\dot{G}_s} (F_s^{[\ell] (k)}, F_s^{[\ell'] (k')}) =\mathbb{C}_{\dot{G}_s^{\varepsilon}} (\delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} F_s^{[\ell] (k)}, F_s^{\varepsilon [\ell'] (k')}) +\mathbb{C}_{\dot{G}_s^{\varepsilon}} (F_s^{\varepsilon' [\ell] (k)}, \delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} F_s^{[\ell'] (k')})\\ \hspace{21em} +\mathbb{C}_{\delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} \dot{G}_s} (F_s^{\varepsilon' [\ell] (k)}, F_s^{\varepsilon' [\ell'] (k')}) \end{array}\)

To estimate \(\| \delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} F \|_{C, \alpha, \beta - \theta, \kappa} := \| F^{\varepsilon} - F^{\varepsilon'} \|_{C, \alpha, \beta - \theta, \kappa}\), we proceed as in the proof of Theorem 4.13, the crucial difference being that \({\delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'}}_{} F_0^{[0]} = 0\) and the estimate \(\| \delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} \dot{G}_t \|_{L^p_{t ; c}} \lesssim 2^{- (d / p - 2 \gamma - \theta) t} \varepsilon^{\theta}\) see Corollary 3.24. In particular, one wants to prove inductively that

\(\displaystyle \| \delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} F^{[\leqslant \ell]} \|_{C, \alpha, \beta, \kappa} \lesssim_{\ell} \varepsilon^{\theta} \sup_{\#= \varepsilon, \varepsilon'} (\| F^{\# [\leqslant \ell]} \|_{C, \alpha - \theta, \beta, \kappa} + \| F^{\# [\leqslant \ell]} \|_{C, \alpha - \theta, \beta, \kappa}^{\ell + 2}) .\)

Repeating the analysis in the proof of Theorem 4.13, we obtain the estimates

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{l} {\| \delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} F_t^{[\ell + 1] (k)} \|_{\mathcal{L}^{(k)}_r (t ; c_{\ell + 1})}}_{}\\ \quad \lesssim \varepsilon^{\theta} C^{k + 1} 2^{(\alpha - \beta k) t} 2^{- \kappa (\ell + 1) t} (\| F^{\# [\leqslant \ell]} \|_{C, \alpha - \theta, \beta, \kappa} + \| F^{\# [\leqslant \ell]} \|_{C, \alpha - \theta, \beta, \kappa}^{\ell + 3}) \end{array}\)

compare with (4.21), implying the claim.\(\Box\)

4.3Global solution of the FBSDE without cutoffs

In this section, we study the global existence and uniqueness of the pair \((\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}, R^{L, \varepsilon})\) solving the coupled equations

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_t & = & - \int_0^t \dot{G}_s^{\varepsilon} (F_s^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s) + R^{\varepsilon}_s) \mathrm{d} s + X_t^{L, \varepsilon},\\ R^{L, \varepsilon}_t & = & \int_t^{\infty} \omega_t (H_{> n ; s}^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s)) \mathrm{d} s + \int_t^{\infty} \omega_t (\mathrm{D} F^{\varepsilon}_s (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s) \cdot \dot{G}_s^{\varepsilon} R^{L, \varepsilon}_s) \mathrm{d} s, \end{array} \) (4.27)

compare with Lemma 3.22, with input \(F_t^{\varepsilon}\) and \(H_{> n ; s}^{\varepsilon}\) graded sequences satisfying suitable smallness assumptions, and then study the limit as the cutoffs \(L \rightarrow \infty\), \(\varepsilon \rightarrow 0\).

To control the global solution of eq. (4.27) and its convergence as \(L \rightarrow \infty\), \(\varepsilon \rightarrow 0\), we use the following topology: for \(\sigma, \sigma' \in \mathbb{R}\), \(a, a' > 0\) and \(\eta \geqslant 0\) we define the norm

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980} (\psi, \psi') \unicode{x2980}_{\ast} := a^{- 1} \sup_{t \in \mathbb{R}^+} 2^{- \sigma t} \| \psi_t \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant n}_t (\eta)} + (a')^{- 1} \sup_{t \in \mathbb{R}^+} 2^{- \sigma' t} \| \varphi_t \|_{L^{\infty} (\eta)}, \) (4.28)

see (3.33) for the definition of \(\| \cdot \|_{L^{\infty} (\eta)}\) and \(\| \cdot \|_{\mathcal{C}^n_t (\eta)}\), where we set

\(\displaystyle \| \psi_t \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant n}_t (\eta)} := \sum_{n' = 0}^n \| \psi_t \|_{\mathcal{C}^n_t (\eta)} .\)

Note that \(\| \cdot \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant n}_t} \equiv \| \cdot \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant n}_t (0)}\) is of course implied.

We can state our result as follows.

Proposition 4.20. Let \(L \in \mathbb{N}_{\infty}\) and \(\varepsilon \in 2^{-\mathbb{N}_{\infty}}\). Let \(\alpha, \beta, \gamma, \kappa\) satisfy (4.14), (4.15) and (4.16), let \(\theta \in [0, (\beta - \gamma) / 2]\) and let \(F^{\varepsilon} = (F^{\varepsilon [\ell] (k)}_t)^{\ell \geqslant 0, k \geqslant 1}_{t \geqslant 0}\) and \(H_{> n}^{\varepsilon} = (H^{\varepsilon [\ell] (k)}_{> n ; t})^{\ell > n, k \geqslant 0}_{t \geqslant 0}\) be the graded sequences in (4.27), with \(n \geqslant \lfloor \alpha \kappa^{- 1} \rfloor + 1\). There exist constants \(D\), \(c_n\), \(\lambda_0 = \lambda_0 (d, \alpha, \beta, \gamma, n)\) such that, if \(\| F^{\varepsilon} \|_{C, \alpha, \beta, \kappa}, \| H_{> n}^{\varepsilon} \|_{C, \alpha, \beta, \kappa} \leqslant \lambda\) for \(C < D^{- 1} / 2\) and \(\lambda \leqslant \lambda_0\) , then (4.27) has a unique global solution \((\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}, R^{L, \varepsilon})\) satisfying

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}, R^{L, \varepsilon}) \unicode{x2980}_{\ast} \leqslant 1 \) (4.29)

with \(\sigma = \gamma + \theta\), \(\sigma' = \lfloor \alpha \kappa^{- 1} \rfloor + 1 - n\), \(a = D\), \(a' = c_n \lambda\) and \(\eta = 0\), these constants appearing in the definition of the norm (4.28).

Remark 4.21. From the fact that \((\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}, R^{L, \varepsilon})\) solves eq. (4.27), which is an equation depending polynomially on \((\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}, R^{L, \varepsilon})\) and on the coupling constant \(\lambda\), we obtain that \(\lambda \mapsto (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}, R^{L, \varepsilon})\) is an analytic function in a neighbourhood of \(0\), uniformly in \(L, \varepsilon\). Indeed, since \((\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}, R^{L, \varepsilon})\) solves a finite-dimensional system which is analytic in all the variables and parameters, the function \(\lambda \mapsto (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}, R^{L, \varepsilon})\) is analytic. Furthermore, defining the power series

\(\displaystyle S^{L, \varepsilon} (\lambda) = \sum_{k = 0}^{+ \infty} \frac{1}{k!} \unicode{x2980} (\partial_{\lambda}^k \Psi^{L, \varepsilon} |_{\lambda = 0}, \partial_{\lambda}^k R^{L, \varepsilon} |_{\lambda = 0}) \unicode{x2980}_{\ast},\)

which is convergent for \(\lambda\) small enough, using the fact that \(F_s^{\varepsilon}\) is a polynomial in \(\Psi\) and \(\lambda\) with norm uniformly bounded in \(\varepsilon\) and \(\lambda\) small enough, we get that \(S^{L, \varepsilon}\) satisfies the differential inequality

\(\displaystyle \frac{\mathrm{d} S^{L, \varepsilon}}{\mathrm{d} \lambda} \leqslant P (S^{L, \varepsilon}, \lambda),\)

where \(P\) is a polynomial with positive coefficients, independent of \(\varepsilon\) and \(L\), but depending on the degree and the bounds on the coefficients of \(F_s^{\varepsilon}\). Following [ABDG20, Appendix C], we have that \(S^{L, \varepsilon}\) has a convergence radius independent of \(L, \varepsilon\), which implies that \(\lambda \mapsto (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}, R^{L, \varepsilon})\) has a convergent radius independent of \(L, \varepsilon\).

By taking the limit as \(\varepsilon \rightarrow 0\) and \(L \rightarrow \infty\), we get that also \(\lambda \mapsto (\Psi_{}, R) = \lim_{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0, L \rightarrow \infty} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}, R^{L, \varepsilon})\) is analytic in a neighbourhood of \(0\). This also implies that the \(n\)-point functions (which are expectations of the form \(\omega (\Psi_{\infty} (f_1) \cdots \Psi_{\infty} (f_n))\)) are analytic for \(\lambda\) in a neighbourhood of \(0\).

The following lemma will be used in the proof of Proposition 4.20.

Lemma 4.22. Let \(N, n, n' \in \mathbb{N}\), let \(F = (F_t^{[\ell] (k)})_{t \geqslant 0}^{N \geqslant \ell > n, k \geqslant n'}\) be a graded sequence and let \(\psi_t, \varphi_t\) be Grassmann fields such that \(\| \psi_t \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t}, \| \varphi_t \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t} \leqslant D 2^{\rho t}\) for some \(D, \rho > 0\). Then, provided that \(\beta > \rho\) and \(C < D^{- 1} / 2\) the following bounds hold true:

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rll} \| F_t (\psi_t) - F_t (\varphi_t) \|_{L^{\infty}} & \lesssim_N & \| F \|_{C, \alpha, \beta, \kappa} 2^{[\alpha - \rho + n' (\rho - \beta) - n \kappa] t} \| \psi_t - \varphi_t \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t}\\ \| \mathrm{D} F_t (\psi_t) - \mathrm{D} F_t (\varphi_t) \|_{\mathcal{B} (L^{\infty} ; L^{\infty})} & \lesssim_N & \| F \|_{C, \alpha, \beta, \kappa} 2^{[\alpha - 2 \rho + n' (\rho - \beta) - n \kappa] t} \| \psi_t - \varphi_t \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t} \end{array}\)

Proof. Note the bounds

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{l} \| F_t^{[\ell] (k)} (\psi_t) - F_t^{[\ell] (k)} (\varphi_t) \|_{L^{\infty}}\\ \qquad \leqslant k \| F \|_{C, \alpha, \beta, \kappa} C^k D^k 2^{(\alpha - \rho) t} 2^{(\rho - \beta) k t} 2^{- \kappa \ell t} \| \psi_t - \varphi_t \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t}, \end{array}\)

and

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{l} \| \mathrm{D} F_t^{[\ell] (k)} (\psi_t) - \mathrm{D} F_t^{[\ell] (k)} (\varphi_t) \|_{\mathcal{B} (L^{\infty} ; L^{\infty})}\\ \qquad \leqslant k (k + 1) \| F \|_{C, \alpha, \beta, \kappa} C^{k + 1} D^k 2^{(\alpha - 2 \rho) t} 2^{(\rho - \beta) (k + 1) t} 2^{- \kappa \ell t} \| \psi_t - \varphi_t \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t} . \end{array}\)

Then, the summation over \(k \geqslant n'\) is controlled by the condition \(CD < 1 / 2\), whereas the summation over \(\ell\) is finite uniformly in \(t \geqslant 0\) but depending on \(N\).\(\Box\)

Proof of Proposition 4.20. The proof is an application of the Banach fixed-point theorem, since we can interpret (4.27) as a fixed-point equation \((\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}, R^{L, \varepsilon}) = \Gamma [(\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}, R^{L, \varepsilon})]\) where the operator \(\Gamma : (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}, R^{L, \varepsilon}) \mapsto (\tilde{\Psi}^{L, \varepsilon}, \tilde{R}^{L, \varepsilon})\) is defined in an obvious way.

We first prove that \(\Gamma\) maps the closed ball \(\unicode{x2980} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}, R^{L, \varepsilon}) \unicode{x2980}_{\ast} \leqslant 1\) into itself. To this end, by using the assumption \(\| F^{\varepsilon} \|_{C, \alpha, \beta, \kappa} \leqslant \lambda\) and that \(\| \Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s} \leqslant D 2^{(\gamma + \theta) s}\) in the said ball, for \(C\) small enough Lemma 4.22 (with \(\varphi = 0\)) gives

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rll} \| F_s^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s) \|_{L^{\infty}} & \leqslant & \tilde{c}_n \lambda 2^{(\alpha - \beta + \gamma + \theta) s}\\ \| \mathrm{D} F_s^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s) \|_{\mathcal{B} (L^{\infty} ; L^{\infty})} & \leqslant & \tilde{c}_n \lambda_{} 2^{(\alpha - \beta) s}\\ \| H_{> n ; s}^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s) \|_{L^{\infty}} & \leqslant & \tilde{c}_n \lambda 2^{(\alpha - n \kappa) s}, \end{array} \) (4.30)

for some constant \(\tilde{c}_n\). To control the equation for the remainder, we need to integrate (4.30) in \(\mathrm{d} s\), which can be done provided that \(n > \alpha \kappa^{- 1}\); actually, for \(n\) sufficiently large we can make the remainder as small as we want. In fact, we have:

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| \tilde{R}^{L, \varepsilon}_t \|_{L^{\infty}} & \leqslant & \int_t^{\infty} \| \omega_t (H_{> n ; s}^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s)) \|_{L^{\infty}} \mathrm{d} s + \int_t^{\infty} \| \omega_t (\mathrm{D} F_s^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s) \cdot \dot{G}_s^{\varepsilon} R^{L, \varepsilon}_s) \|_{L^{\infty}} \mathrm{d} s\\ & \leqslant & \int_t^{\infty } \| H_{> n ; s}^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s) \| \mathrm{d} s + \int_t^{\infty} \| \mathrm{D} F_s^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s) \|_{\mathcal{B} (L^{\infty} ; L^{\infty})} \cdot \| \dot{G}_s^{\varepsilon} \|_{L^1} \| R^{L, \varepsilon}_s \|_{L^{\infty}} \mathrm{d} s\\ & \lesssim & \tilde{c}_n \lambda 2^{- \sigma' t} + \tilde{c}_n \lambda \int_t^{\infty} 2^{- (d - 2 \gamma - \alpha + \beta) s} \| R^{L, \varepsilon}_s \|_{L^{\infty}} \mathrm{d} s, \end{array} \) (4.31)

where we used (4.30) and that \(\| \dot{G}_s^{\varepsilon} \|_{L^1} \lesssim 2^{- (d - 2 \gamma) s}\) and that \(n \kappa - \alpha \geqslant 1\). Since in the ball \(\unicode{x2980} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}, R^{L, \varepsilon}) \unicode{x2980}_{\ast} \leqslant 1\) and since \(d - 2 \gamma - \alpha + \beta > 0\), we have \(\int_t^{\infty} 2^{- (d - 2 \gamma - \alpha + \beta) s} \| R^{L, \varepsilon}_s \|_{L^{\infty}} \mathrm{d} s \leqslant c_n \lambda 2^{- \sigma' t} (d - 2 \gamma - \alpha + \beta)^{- 1}\), hence, for \(c_n\) large enough and for \(\lambda\) small enough

\(\displaystyle (c_n \lambda)^{- 1} \sup_{t \in \mathbb{R}^+} 2^{\sigma' t} \| \tilde{R}^{L, \varepsilon}_t \|_{L^{\infty}} \mathrm{d} s \leqslant \frac{1}{2} + \tilde{c}_n \lambda (d - 2 \gamma - \alpha + \beta)^{- 1} < 1. \) (4.32)

Next, by Lemma 3.26 we have the estimate \(\| X^{L, \varepsilon}_t \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t} \lesssim 2^{\gamma t}\), uniformly in \(L, \varepsilon\). Therefore, we write:

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| \tilde{\Psi}^{L, \varepsilon}_s \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t} & \leqslant & \int_0^t \sum_{0 \leqslant n' \leqslant 2} \sum_{\nu} \| (2^{- n' t} \partial_{\nu}^{n'} \dot{G}_s^{\varepsilon}) (F_s^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s) + R^{L, \varepsilon}_s) \|_{L^{\infty}} \mathrm{d} s + \| X^{L, \varepsilon}_t \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t}\\ & \lesssim & \int_0^t 2^{- (d - 2 \gamma) s} (\| F_s^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s) \|_{L^{\infty}} + \| R^{L, \varepsilon}_s \|_{L^{\infty}}) \mathrm{d} s + 2^{\gamma t}\\ & \lesssim & \tilde{c}_n \lambda \int_0^t 2^{- (d - 3 \gamma - \alpha + \beta - \theta) s} \mathrm{d} s + 2^{\gamma t}\\ & \lesssim & \tilde{c}_n \lambda 2^{(\gamma + \theta) t} + 2^{\gamma t}, \end{array} \) (4.33)

where we used (4.30), the propagator estimates in Corollary 3.24, the assumption on \(R^{L, \varepsilon}\) and again that \(d - 2 \gamma - \alpha + \beta > 0\). Therefore, putting together (4.32) and (4.33), for \(D\) large enough and \(\lambda\) small enough we have that the closed unit ball is left invariant by \(\Gamma\), that is \(\unicode{x2980} (\tilde{\Psi}^{L, \varepsilon}, \tilde{R}^{L, \varepsilon}) \unicode{x2980}_{\ast} \leqslant 1\).

We now prove that \(\Gamma\) is a contraction. To this end, assume that \((\tilde{\Psi}^{L, \varepsilon}, \tilde{R}^{L, \varepsilon})\) is another solution in the said ball and write the difference of the two solutions

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rll} \Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_t - \tilde{\Psi}^{L, \varepsilon}_t & = & - \int_0^t \dot{G}_s^{\varepsilon} (F_s^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s) - F_s^{\varepsilon} (\tilde{\Psi}^{L, \varepsilon}_s) + R^{L, \varepsilon}_s - \tilde{R}^{L, \varepsilon}_s) \mathrm{d} s,\\ R^{L, \varepsilon}_t - \tilde{R}^{L, \varepsilon}_t & = & \int_t^{\infty} \omega_t (H_{> n ; s}^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s) - H_{> n ; s}^{\varepsilon} (\tilde{\Psi}^{L, \varepsilon}_s)) \mathrm{d} s\\ & & \quad + \int_t^{\infty} \omega_t (\mathrm{D} F_s^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s) \cdot \dot{G}_s^{\varepsilon} (R^{L, \varepsilon}_s - \tilde{R}^{L, \varepsilon}_s)) \mathrm{d} s\\ & & \hspace{3em} + \int_t^{\infty} \omega_t ((\mathrm{D} F_s^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s) - \mathrm{D} F_s^{\varepsilon} (\tilde{\Psi}^{L, \varepsilon}_s)) \cdot \dot{G}_s^{\varepsilon} \tilde{R}^{L, \varepsilon}_s) \mathrm{d} s. \end{array} \) (4.34)

By using the assumption \(\| F^{\varepsilon} \|_{C, \alpha, \beta, \kappa} \leqslant \lambda\) as well as \({\| \Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s}}_{}, {\| \tilde{\Psi}^{L, \varepsilon}_s \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s}}_{} \leqslant D 2^{(\gamma + \theta) s}\), Lemma 4.22 implies

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rll} \| F_s^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s) - F_s^{\varepsilon} (\tilde{\Psi}^{L, \varepsilon}_s) \|_{L^{\infty}} & \leqslant & \tilde{c}_n \lambda 2^{(\alpha - \beta) s} {\| \Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s - \tilde{\Psi}^{L, \varepsilon}_s \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s}}_{},\\ \| \mathrm{D} F_s^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s) - \mathrm{D} F_s^{\varepsilon} (\tilde{\Psi}^{L, \varepsilon}_s) \|_{\mathcal{B} (L^{\infty} ; L^{\infty})} & \leqslant & \tilde{c}_n \lambda 2^{(\alpha - \beta - \gamma - \theta) s} {\| \Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s - \tilde{\Psi}^{L, \varepsilon}_s \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s}}_{},\\ \| H_{> n ; s}^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s) - H_{> n ; s}^{\varepsilon} (\tilde{\Psi}^{L, \varepsilon}_s) \|_{L^{\infty}} & \leqslant & \tilde{c}_n \lambda 2^{(\alpha - \gamma - \theta - n \kappa) s} {\| \Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s - \tilde{\Psi}^{L, \varepsilon}_s \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s}}_{} . \end{array} \) (4.35)

Therefore, plugging these estimates together with (4.30) into (4.34) we obtain:

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & {\| \Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_t - \tilde{\Psi}^{L, \varepsilon}_t \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t}}_{}\\ \, & \quad \lesssim \tilde{c}_n \lambda 2^{(\gamma + \theta) t} (d - 2 \gamma - \alpha + \beta)^{- 1} \sup_{s \in \mathbb{R}^+} 2^{- (\gamma + \theta) s} {\| \Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s - \tilde{\Psi}^{L, \varepsilon}_s \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s}}_{}\\ & \hspace{5em} + (d - 2 \gamma + \sigma')^{- 1} \sup_{s \in \mathbb{R}^+} 2^{\sigma' s} \| R^{L, \varepsilon}_s - \tilde{R}^{L, \varepsilon}_s \|_{L^{\infty}}, \end{array} \) (4.36)

and

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{l} \| R^{L, \varepsilon}_t - \tilde{R}^{L, \varepsilon}_t \|_{L^{\infty}}\\ \quad \lesssim \tilde{c}_n \lambda 2^{- \sigma' t} \sup_{s \in \mathbb{R}^+} 2^{- (\gamma + \theta) s} {\| \Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s - \tilde{\Psi}^{L, \varepsilon}_s \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s}}_{}\\ \hspace{3em} + \tilde{c}_n \lambda 2^{- \sigma' t} (d - 2 \gamma - \alpha + \beta + \sigma')^{- 1} \sup_{s \in \mathbb{R}^+} 2^{\sigma' s} \| R^{L, \varepsilon}_s - \tilde{R}^{L, \varepsilon}_s \|_{L^{\infty}}\\ \hspace{3em} + \tilde{c}_n c_n \lambda^2 (d - 2 \gamma - \alpha + \beta + \sigma') 2^{- \sigma' s} \sup_{s \in \mathbb{R}^+} 2^{- (\gamma + \theta) s} {\| \Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s - \tilde{\Psi}^{L, \varepsilon}_s \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s}}_{}, \end{array} \) (4.37)

which for \(c_n\) large enough and \(\lambda\) small enough, imply the claim.\(\Box\)

Proof of Lemma 3.22. The previous proof implies that Picard's iteration converges to the solution \(\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_t\). Since \(X^{L, \varepsilon}_t (x + m \hspace{1em} L) = X^{L, \varepsilon}_t (x)\), see Definition 3.20, one can check that every \(n\)-th Picard's iterate \(\Psi^{(n) L, \varepsilon}_t\) is periodic. In fact, if \(\Psi^{(n) L, \varepsilon}_t\) is periodic, so are \(F^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{(n) L, \varepsilon}_t)\) and \(\mathfrak{\dot{g}}^{\varepsilon}_t F^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{(n) L, \varepsilon}_t)\) because of translation invariance, implying that also \(\Psi^{(n + 1) L, \varepsilon}_t\) is periodic.\(\Box\)

As a consequence of Proposition 4.20, we have that the sequence \((\Psi^{L, \varepsilon})_{L, \varepsilon}\) is bounded in the topology \(\| \cdot \|_{\gamma, 0}\), see (1.9), introduced before Theorem 1.1. We now address the convergence of the sequence \((\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}, R^{L, \varepsilon})\) as \(L \rightarrow \infty\) and \(\varepsilon \rightarrow 0\). We first prove the convergence to the continuum limit, \((\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}, R^{L, \varepsilon}) \rightarrow (\Psi^L, R^L)\).

Proposition 4.23. (Continuum limit). Let \(L \in \mathbb{N}_{\infty}\) and \(\varepsilon > \varepsilon' \in 2^{-\mathbb{N}_{\infty}}\). Under the same assumptions of Proposition 4.20, but with \(\alpha > 3 \beta\), \(0 < \theta \leqslant \min \{ \alpha - 3 \beta ; \beta - \gamma \} / 2\), there exist \(D_{\theta}\), \(c_n\) and \(\lambda = \lambda (d, \alpha, \beta, \gamma, n)\) such that, if \(\| F^{\varepsilon} \|_{C, \alpha - \theta, \beta, \kappa}, \| F^{\varepsilon'} \|_{C, \alpha - \theta, \beta, \kappa}, \| H_{> n}^{\varepsilon} \|_{C, \alpha - \theta, \beta, \kappa}, \| H_{> n}^{\varepsilon'} \|_{C, \alpha - \theta, \beta, \kappa} \leqslant \lambda\) for \(C < D_{\theta}^{- 1} / 2\) , then uniformly in \(L\)

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon} - \Psi^{L, \varepsilon'}, R^{L, \varepsilon} - R^{L, \varepsilon'}) \unicode{x2980}_{\ast} \lesssim \varepsilon^{\theta},\)

with \(\sigma = \gamma + \theta\), \(\sigma' = \lfloor \alpha \kappa^{- 1} \rfloor + 1 - n\), \(a = D_{\theta}\), \(a' = c_n \lambda\) and \(\eta = 0\), these constants appearing in the definition of the norm (4.28).

Proof. The equation for the difference is similar to eq. (4.34), but involves the differences \(\delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} F_s := F_s^{\varepsilon} - F_s^{\varepsilon'}\), \(\delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} \dot{G}_s := \dot{G}_s^{\varepsilon} - \dot{G}_s^{\varepsilon'}\) and \(\delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} X^L_t := X^{L, \varepsilon}_t - X^{L, \varepsilon'}_t\) in an obvious way. We control such differences by Corollary 3.24, Corollary 3.26, and Proposition 4.19:

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rll} \| \delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} \dot{G}_t \|_{L^p_t} & \lesssim & 2^{- (d / p - 2 \gamma - \theta) t} \varepsilon^{\theta},\\ \| \delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} F \|_{C, \alpha, \beta, \kappa} & \leqslant & \tilde{c}_n \varepsilon^{\theta},\\ \| \delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} X^L_t \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t} & \lesssim & 2^{(\gamma + \theta) t} \varepsilon^{\theta}, \end{array} \) (4.38)

for some constant \(\tilde{c}_n\). Therefore, we can follow the analysis after (4.34) to obtain the same estimates but with the extra gain \(\varepsilon^{\theta}\) and thus prove the claim.\(\Box\)

To prove convergence to the infinite-volume limit \((\Psi^L, R^L) \rightarrow (\Psi, R)\), we need to take \(\eta > 0\) in \(\unicode{x2980} \cdot \unicode{x2980}_{\ast}\).

Proposition 4.24. (Infinite-volume limit). Let \(L < L' \in \mathbb{N}_{\infty}\) and \(\varepsilon \in 2^{-\mathbb{N}_{\infty}}\). Under the same assumptions of Proposition 4.20 and for any \(\eta > 0\), there exist constants \(D_{\eta}\), \(c_n\) and \(\lambda = \lambda (d, \alpha, \beta, \gamma, n)\) such that, if \(\| F^{\varepsilon} \|_{C, \alpha, \beta, \kappa}, \| H_{> n}^{\varepsilon} \|_{C, \alpha, \beta, \kappa}, \leqslant \lambda\) for \(C < D_{\eta}^{- 1} / 2\) , then uniformly in \(\varepsilon\)

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980} (\Psi^{L', \varepsilon} - \Psi^{L, \varepsilon}, R^{L', \varepsilon} - R^{L, \varepsilon}) \unicode{x2980}_{\ast} \lesssim L^{- \eta},\)

with \(\sigma = \gamma + \theta\), \(\sigma' = \lfloor \alpha \kappa^{- 1} \rfloor + 1 - n\), \(a = D_{\eta}\), \(a' = c_n \lambda\), these constants appearing in the definition of the norm (4.28).

We skip the proof of this proposition because the analysis of the next section carries out a similar argument in a more complex setting. See also [ABDG20] for similar arguments in the context of the infinite-volume limit of Grassmann SPDEs.

So far we have established the existence of the field \(\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}\) and its convergence in a suitable topology for distributional fields on \(\mathbb{R}^+ \times \mathbb{R}^d\) as the cutoffs are removed, \(L \rightarrow \infty\) and \(\varepsilon \rightarrow 0\). We shall now consider the properties of the sequence \((\Psi_t^{L, \varepsilon})\) for fixed \(t\) in suitable Besov spaces.

Corollary 4.25. With the same setting of Proposition 4.20, for any \(L' \geqslant L \in \mathbb{N}_{\infty}\), \(\varepsilon \geqslant \varepsilon' \in 2^{-\mathbb{N}_{\infty}}\), \(\rho > 0\) and \(t \in [0, \infty]\)

\(\displaystyle \| \Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_t \|_{B^{- \gamma - \rho}_{\infty, \infty}} \lesssim \rho^{- 1 / 2}, \hspace{3em} \| \Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_t - \Psi^{L', \varepsilon'}_t \|_{B^{- \gamma - \theta - \rho}_{\infty, \infty, \eta}} \lesssim \rho^{- 1 / 2} (\varepsilon^{\theta} \boldsymbol{1}_{\varepsilon > \varepsilon'} + L^{- \eta} \boldsymbol{1}_{L' > L}) . \) (4.39)

Proof. Denote by \((\Delta_j)_{j \geqslant - 1}\) the Littlewood–Paley blocks. It suffices to bound \(\| \Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_t (\Delta_j) \|\) in the fixed-point equation (4.27) by noting that the function \(\dot{G}_s^{\varepsilon} \Delta_j\) satisfies the estimate

\(\displaystyle \| \dot{G}_s^{\varepsilon} \Delta_j (\cdot - x) \|_{L^1} \lesssim 2^{- (d - \gamma) s} 2^{\gamma j} \| \Delta_j \|_{L^1} = 2^{- (d - \gamma) s} 2^{\gamma j},\)

and by recalling that \(\| X^{L, \varepsilon}_t (\Delta_j (\cdot - x)) \| \lesssim 2^{(\gamma + \rho) j}\), for any \(\rho > 0\), see Corollary 3.26. Accordingly, for any \(L \in \mathbb{N}_{\infty}\), \(\varepsilon \in 2^{-\mathbb{N}_{\infty}}\) and \(t \geqslant 0\) we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| \Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_t (\Delta_j (\cdot - x)) \| & \lesssim & \int_0^t \| \dot{G}_s^{\varepsilon} \Delta_j (\cdot - x) \|_{L^1} \| (F_s^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s) + R^{L, \varepsilon}_s) \|_{L^{\infty}} \mathrm{d} s + \| X^{L, \varepsilon}_t (\Delta_j (\cdot - x)) \|\\ & \lesssim & 2^{\gamma j} \int_0^t 2^{- (d - \gamma) s} \| (F_s^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s) + R^{L, \varepsilon}_s) \|_{L^{\infty}} \mathrm{d} s + 2^{(\gamma + \rho) j}\\ & \lesssim_{\rho} & 2^{(\gamma + \rho) j}, \end{array} \) (4.40)

where we used that \(d - 2 \gamma - \alpha + \beta > 0\). To prove the other bound, we first consider the equation for the difference

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{l} \Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_t (\Delta_j (\cdot - x)) - \Psi_t^{L, \varepsilon'} (\Delta_j (\cdot - x))\\ \qquad = \int_0^t [\Delta_j (\cdot - x) \dot{G}_s^{\varepsilon} (F_s^{\varepsilon} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_s) + R^{L, \varepsilon}_s) - \Delta_j (\cdot - x) \dot{G}_s^{\varepsilon'} (F_s^{\varepsilon'} (\Psi^{L, \varepsilon'}_s) + R^{L, \varepsilon'}_s)] \mathrm{d} s\\ \hspace{10em} + X^{L, \varepsilon}_t (\Delta_j (\cdot - x)) - X^{L, \varepsilon'}_t (\Delta_j (\cdot - x)) . \end{array}\)

and write the integrand in terms of the differences \(\delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} F_s\), \(\delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} \dot{G}_s\), \(\Psi^{L, \varepsilon} - \Psi^{L, \varepsilon'}\) and \(R^{L, \varepsilon} - R^{L, \varepsilon'}\). The estimates for \(\delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} F_s\) and \(\delta_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon'} \dot{G}_s\) are provided in (4.38), the estimates for \(\| \Psi^{L, \varepsilon} - \Psi^{L, \varepsilon'} \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s}\) and for \(\| R^{L, \varepsilon} - R^{L, \varepsilon'} \|_{L^{\infty}}\) in Proposition 4.23 whereas the estimate \(\| X^{L, \varepsilon}_t (\Delta_j (\cdot - x)) - X^{L, \varepsilon'}_t (\Delta_j (\cdot - x)) \| \lesssim_{\rho} \varepsilon^{\theta} 2^{(\gamma + \theta + \rho) j}\) is provided in Corollary 3.26. Carrying out the steps as in (4.40) we gain the extra \(\varepsilon^{\theta}\) in the drift and thus prove that

\(\displaystyle \| \Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_t - \Psi^{L, \varepsilon'}_t \|_{B^{- \gamma - \theta - \rho}_{\infty, \infty}} \lesssim_{\rho} \varepsilon^{\theta} . \) (4.41)

We then consider the equation for the other difference \(\Psi^{L', \varepsilon}_t (\Delta_j (\cdot - x)) - \Psi_t^{L, \varepsilon} (\Delta_j (\cdot - x))\) and expand it in the differences \(\Psi^{L', \varepsilon} - \Psi^{L, \varepsilon}\) and \(R^{L', \varepsilon} - R^{L, \varepsilon}\) which are estimated by Proposition 4.24 in the \(\| \cdot \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t (\eta)}\) and \(\| \cdot \|_{L^{\infty} (\eta)}\) norms respectively. Since \(\| X^{L', \varepsilon}_t (\Delta_j (\cdot - x)) - X^{L, \varepsilon}_t (\Delta_j (\cdot - x)) \| \lesssim_{\rho} L^{- \eta} 2^{(\gamma + \rho) j}\), see Corollary 3.26, following the same strategy used above, we have

\(\displaystyle \| \Psi^{L, \varepsilon}_t - \Psi^{L, \varepsilon'}_t \|_{B^{- \gamma - \rho}_{\infty, \infty, \eta}} \lesssim_{\rho} L^{- \eta},\)

which, together with (4.41) implies (4.39).\(\Box\)

Proof Theorem 1.1. The global existence of \(\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}\) for \(L \in \mathbb{N}\) and \(\varepsilon \in 2^{-\mathbb{N}}\) follows by Proposition 4.20. In fact, having fixed \(\gamma\), choose some \(\alpha, \beta, \kappa\) such the constraints in (4.14), (4.15), (4.16) are satisfied. Choose \(n = \lfloor \alpha \kappa^{- 1} \rfloor + 2\) (so that \(\gamma < \sigma'\)) and \(C \leqslant C_0 (\alpha, \beta, \gamma, \kappa, n) \wedge D^{- 1} / 2\) and (by abuse of notation) \(\lambda_0 (d, \gamma) := 1 \wedge \lambda_0 (C) \wedge \lambda_0 (d, \alpha, \beta, \gamma)\), where \(C_0\) and \(\lambda_0 (C)\) are as in Corollary 4.15, whereas \(D\) and \(\lambda_0 (d, \alpha, \beta, \gamma)\) from Proposition 4.20. By Theorem 4.13 there exists a unique global solution of the \(n\)-truncated equation with boundary datum \(F^{\varepsilon [0]}_0 (\psi_t) := \lambda (\psi_t)^3\) for \(\lambda \leqslant \lambda_0 (d, \gamma)\). Denote by \(\mu_{\infty}^{\varepsilon} (\lambda) \equiv \mu_{\infty}^{\varepsilon} (F^{\varepsilon [0]}_0)\) the function defined in Definition 4.17 and note that \(F_{\infty}^{\varepsilon} = \mathrm{D} V_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}\) provided that \(\mu^{\varepsilon} (\lambda) = \mu^{\varepsilon}_{\infty} (\lambda)\). By Corollary 4.15, \(F^{\varepsilon}\) and \(H^{\varepsilon}_{> n}\) satisfy the assumptions of Proposition 4.20, which for \(\theta = 0\) and for any \(L \in \mathbb{N}\) and \(\varepsilon \in 2^{-\mathbb{N}}\) implies the existence of a unique pair \((\Psi^{L, \varepsilon}, R^{L, \varepsilon})\) solving (4.27) and by Lemma 3.14 also the FBSDE which was the goal.

By combining Proposition 4.23 with Proposition 4.24 we have the existence of the limit \(\Psi = \lim_{L \rightarrow \infty} \lim_{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \Psi^{L, \varepsilon}\) and the bound (1.10). Finally, (1.11) is a direct consequence of Corollary 4.25. \(\Box\)

Remark 4.26. Note that the existence of the weak limit \(\omega^V\) stated in Theorem 1.3 follows by Theorem 1.1, by duality \(\|^{} (\Psi_{\infty} - \Psi_{\infty}^{L, \varepsilon}) (f) \| \lesssim \| f \|_{B^{\gamma + \theta}_{1, 1, - \eta}} \| \Psi_{\infty} - \Psi_{\infty}^{L, \varepsilon} \|_{B^{- \gamma - \theta}_{\infty, \infty, \eta}}\) and by the stochastic quantisation formula of Theorem 3.12.

4.4Exponential decay and short-distance divergence of correlations

In this section, we prove the exponential decay of correlation functions via a coupling method and the short-distance divergence of the two-point correlation function by using a generating function. We learned the coupling method from the work [Fun91] and more recently explored in [GHR22] in the context of stochastic quantisation. For the sake of simplicity, we shall restrict ourselves to the two-cluster correlation function. The basic idea is to approximate the solution of the FBSDE in suitably disjoint spatial domains by two independent Grassmann processes that solve some other FBSDEs with uncorrelated GBMs as sources. Because the latter are related to a massive operator, one expects that the approximation can be made “exponentially accurate”, depending on the distance of the regions. This technique can be viewed as a sensible replacement of the well-known cluster expansions [Bry84, GJ87] of statistical mechanics, applied in the context of stochastic quantisation in [Dim90] .

Let us discuss the coupling method in more detail. Consider \(((f^{(i, k)})_{k = 1}^{m_i})_{i = 1}^2 \subset B^{\gamma^+}_{1, 1} (\mathbb{R}^d ; \mathbb{C}^4)\) compactly supported, define \(D_i := \bigcup_{k = 1}^{m_i} \operatorname{supp} (f^{(i, k)})\), and assume that \(D_1 \cap D_2 = \varnothing\). We let \(\mathcal{D}_i \supset D_i\) be such that \(\mathcal{D}_1 \cap \mathcal{D}_2 = \varnothing\) and such that \(\sup_i \operatorname{dist} (\mathcal{D}_i^c, D_i)\) is as large as possible, where \(\mathcal{D}_i^c := \mathbb{R}^d \setminus \mathcal{D}_i\). For brevity, we will here consider the case \(L = \infty\) and \(\varepsilon = 0\) directly. Recall that \(\dot{G}_t = \mathfrak{C}_t^2 U\), with \([\mathfrak{C}_t, U] = [\mathfrak{C}_t, \Theta] = 0\).

Definition 4.27. We let \((X^{(i)}_t)^{i = 0, 1, 2}_t\) be the family of anticommuting norm-compatible GBMs such that, setting \(\mathcal{D}_0 := \mathbb{R}^d\),

\(\displaystyle \omega (X^{(i)}_t (f) X^{(j)}_t (g)) = \left\llangle f, \int_0^t \mathfrak{C}_t \boldsymbol{1}_{\mathcal{D}_i} \boldsymbol{1}_{\mathcal{D}_j} \mathfrak{C}_t U g \mathrm{d} s \right\rrangle_{\Theta} \qquad \forall f, g \in \mathfrak{h} .\) (4.42)

As anticipated, the process \((X^{(0)}_t)_t := (X_t)_t\) is the diffusion of the FBSDEs considered so far, whereas \((X^{(i)}_t)_t\), \(i = 1, 2\), are independent local approximations of the latter in the region \(D_i\). The independence is read out from (4.42), which, by the choice of \(\mathcal{D}_i\) implies \(\omega (X^{(1)}_t (f) X^{(2)}_t (g)) = 0\), for any \(f, g \in \mathfrak{h}\).

If \(\psi\) is a field on \(\mathbb{R}^d\), that is, \(\mathbb{R}^d \ni x \mapsto (\psi_{\mu} (x))_{\mu}\), we introduce the weighted topologies

\(\displaystyle \| \psi \|_{L^{\infty}_t (\mathcal{D} ; B)} := \sup_{x \in \mathbb{R}^d} \mathrm{e}^{- \xi 2^t \operatorname{dist} (x, B)} \mathrm{e}^{\xi \operatorname{dist} (x, \mathcal{D})} \| \psi_{\mu} (x) \|, \) (4.43)
\(\displaystyle \| \psi \|_{\mathcal{C}^n_t (\mathcal{D} ; B)} := 2^{- nt} \sup_{\nu} \| \partial^n_{\nu} \psi \|_{L^{\infty}_t (\mathcal{D} ; B)}, \) (4.44)

where \(t \geqslant 0\), where \(\xi > 0\) is small enough, and where \(B, \mathcal{D} \subset \mathbb{R}^d\) are measurable domains. We also set \(\| \cdot \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant n}_t (\mathcal{D} ; B)} := \sum_{0 \leqslant n' \leqslant n} \| \cdot \|_{\mathcal{C}^n_t (\mathcal{D} ; B)}\). If \(B =\mathbb{R}^d\), then we abridge our notation to \(\| \cdot \|_{\mathcal{C}^n_t (\mathcal{D})}\), \(\| \cdot \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant n}_t (\mathcal{D})}\) and \(\| \cdot \|_{L^{\infty} (\mathcal{D})}\), the right-hand side of the latter being independent of \(t\).

Lemma 4.28. Let \((X_t^{(i)})^i_t\), \((D_i)_i\) and \((\mathcal{D}_i)_i\) be as above. Then, for \(\xi > 0\) small enough, uniformly in \(t \geqslant 0\)

\(\displaystyle \| X_t - X_t^{(i)} \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant n}_t (\mathcal{D}^c_i ; D_i)} \lesssim \operatorname{dist} (D_i, \mathcal{D}_i^c)^{- \gamma} \mathrm{e}^{- \xi \operatorname{dist} (D_i, \mathcal{D}_i^c)} . \) (4.45)

Proof. We note that the derivative in \(t\) of the covariance of \(X_t - X_t^{(i)}\) is \(\mathfrak{C}_t \boldsymbol{1}_{\mathcal{D}_i^c} \mathfrak{C}_t U = (\boldsymbol{1}_{\mathcal{D}_i^c} \mathfrak{C}_t)^{\ast} \boldsymbol{1}_{\mathcal{D}_i^c} \mathfrak{C}_t U\). Accordingly, by the norm compatibility we can write

\(\displaystyle \| (\partial^n_{\nu} X_t - \partial^n_{\nu} X_t^{(i)}) (x) \|^2 \lesssim \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \int_0^t | \partial^n_{\nu} \mathfrak{C}_s (z ; x) |^2 \boldsymbol{1}_{\mathcal{D}_i^c} (z) \mathrm{d} z\operatorname{ds},\)

where \(\lesssim\) is up to some universal constant. Therefore, by using the estimate \(| \partial^{\nu}_x \mathfrak{C}_s (z ; x) | \lesssim 2^{(\gamma + d / 2 + n) s} \mathrm{e}^{- \bar{c} 2^s | z - x |}\), see Remark 3.25, we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{l} 2^{- nt} \mathrm{e}^{- \xi 2^t \operatorname{dist} (x, D_i)} \mathrm{e}^{\xi \operatorname{dist} (x, \mathcal{D}^c_i)} \| (\partial^n_{\nu} X_t - \partial^n_{\nu} X_t^{(i)}) (x) \|\\ \; \lesssim 2^{- nt} \mathrm{e}^{- \xi 2^t \operatorname{dist} (x, D_i)} \mathrm{e}^{\xi \operatorname{dist} (x, \mathcal{D}^c_i)} \left( \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \int_0^t | \partial^n_{\nu} \mathfrak{C}_s (z ; x) |^2 \boldsymbol{1}_{\mathcal{D}_i^c} (z) \mathrm{d} s\operatorname{dz} \right)^{1 / 2}\\ \; \lesssim 2^{- nt} \left[ \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \int_0^t 2^{(2 \gamma + d + 2 n) s} \mathrm{e}^{- \xi 2^s \operatorname{dist} (x, D_i)} \mathrm{e}^{- (\bar{c} 2^s - \xi) | z - x |} \boldsymbol{1}_{\mathcal{D}_i^c} (z) \mathrm{d} s\operatorname{dz} \right]^{1 / 2}\\ \; \leqslant \left[ \int_0^t 2^{(2 \gamma + d) s} \mathrm{e}^{- \xi 2^s \operatorname{dist} (x, D_i)} \mathrm{e}^{- \bar{c} 2^s \operatorname{dist} (x, \mathcal{D}_i^c) / 3} \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \mathrm{e}^{- \bar{c} 2^s | z - x | / 3} \mathrm{d} z\operatorname{ds} \right]^{1 / 2}\\ \; \lesssim \left[ \int_0^t 2^{2 \gamma s} \mathrm{e}^{- \xi 2^s \operatorname{dist} (D_i, \mathcal{D}_i^c)} \operatorname{ds} \right]^{1 / 2},\\ \; \lesssim_{\xi} \operatorname{dist} (D_i, \mathcal{D}_i^c)^{- \gamma} \mathrm{e}^{- \xi \operatorname{dist} (D_i, \mathcal{D}_i^c)}, \end{array} \) (4.46)

provided that \(\xi \leqslant \bar{c} / 3\), hence the claim.\(\Box\)

Let \((\Psi^{(i)})_{i = 0, 1, 2}\) be the Grassmann processes that solve the FBSDEs with \((X^{(i)})_{i = 0, 1, 2}\) as sources. More precisely, one should think of the pairs \((\Psi^{(i)}_t, R_t^{(i)})\), see Lemma 3.22, which solve a system of equations that are always well-defined, unlike the FBSDE.

Clearly, also \((\Psi^{(i)})_{i = 1, 2}\) are independent and one should think of \(\Psi^{(i)}\) as approximating \(\Psi := \Psi^{(0)}\) in the regions \(D_i\) respectively. This allows for the representation.

Lemma 4.29. Let \(F, F' \in \bigoplus_{j = 0}^n (B^{\gamma^+}_{1, 1, 0^-} (\mathbb{R}^d ; \mathbb{C}^4))^{\wedge j}\), for some \(n \in \mathbb{N}\), and let \(X\), \(\Psi\), \(\Psi^{(i)}\) be as above. Then

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{l} \operatorname{Cov}_{\omega^V} (F (X_{\infty}) ; F' (X_{\infty}))\\ \qquad =\operatorname{Cov}_{\omega} (F (\Psi_{\infty}) - F (\Psi_{\infty}^{(1)}) ; F' (\Psi_{\infty})) +\operatorname{Cov}_{\omega} (F (\Psi_{\infty}^{(1)}) ; F' (\Psi_{\infty}) - F' (\Psi_{\infty}^{(2)})) \end{array}\)

Proof. First of all, note that \(F (X_{\infty}), F' (X_{\infty}) \in \mathcal{A}\), where \(\mathcal{A}\) was defined in (1.12), since in fact \(X_{\infty} \equiv \psi\). By the stochastic quantisation formula in Theorem 3.12 and by Corollary 4.25

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lcl} \operatorname{Cov}_{\omega^V} (F (X_{\infty}) ; F' (X_{\infty})) & = & \lim_{L \rightarrow \infty} \lim_{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \operatorname{Cov}_{\omega^V} (F (X_{\infty}^{L, \varepsilon}) ; F' (X_{\infty}^{L, \varepsilon}))\\ & = & \lim_{L \rightarrow \infty} \lim_{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \operatorname{Cov}_{\omega} (F (\Psi_{\infty}^{L, \varepsilon}) ; F' (\Psi_{\infty}^{L, \varepsilon}))^{}\\ & = & \operatorname{Cov}_{\omega} (F (\Psi_{\infty}) ; F' (\Psi_{\infty})) . \end{array}\)

Note in particular that \(F (X_{\infty}^{L, \varepsilon})\) and \(F' (X_{\infty}^{L, \varepsilon})\) are elements of a finite-dimensional Grassmann algebra. Since \(\Psi^{(1)}\) and \(\Psi^{(2)}\) are independent, \(\operatorname{Cov}_{\omega} (F (\Psi_{\infty}^{(1)}) ; F' (\Psi_{\infty}^{(2)})) = 0\) and the claim follows by simple manipulations.\(\Box\)

This lemma reduces the problem to controlling, e.g., \(| \operatorname{Cov}_{\omega} (F (\Psi_{\infty}) - F (\Psi_{\infty}^{(1)}) ; F' (\Psi_{\infty})) |\). We also need another technical lemma, which is a generalisation of Lemma 4.22. Note that because we work without cut-offs, in particular with \(\varepsilon = 0\), we work with exponential weights, compare with (4.44). In particular, the spaces \(\mathcal{L}^{(k)}_t (s ; c)\) should be intended with the exponential weights \(w^{(k)}_{t ; c}\) with \(\delta = 1\). For simplicity, we denote by \(\mathcal{B}_{\xi} (L^{\infty} ; L^{\infty})\) the weighted space \(\mathcal{L}^{(1, 0)}_0 (0 ; \xi)\).

Lemma 4.30. In the same setting of Lemma 4.22 the following bounds hold true, for \(\xi\) small enough

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rll} \| \mathrm{D} F_t (\psi_t) \|_{\mathcal{B}_{\xi} (L^{\infty} ; L^{\infty})} & \lesssim_N & \| F \|_{C, \alpha, \beta, \kappa} 2^{[\alpha - \rho + n' (\rho - \beta) - n \kappa] t}\\ \| F_t (\psi_t) - F_t (\varphi_t) \|_{L^{\infty} (\mathcal{D} ; B)} & \lesssim_N & \| F \|_{C, \alpha, \beta, \kappa} 2^{[\alpha - \rho + n' (\rho - \beta) - n \kappa] t} \| \psi_t - \varphi_t \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t (\mathcal{D} ; B)}\\ \| \mathrm{D} F_t (\psi_t) - \mathrm{D} F_t (\varphi_t) \|_{\mathcal{B} (L^{\infty} ; L^{\infty} (\mathcal{D} ; B))} & \lesssim_N & \| F \|_{C, \alpha, \beta, \kappa} 2^{[\alpha - 2 \rho + n' (\rho - \beta) - n \kappa] t} \| \psi_t - \varphi_t \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t (\mathcal{D} ; B)} . \end{array}\)

Proof. The first bound follows by the same argument of Lemma 4.22. Regarding the other two bounds, we have to suitably distribute the weight. For example, we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{l} \| F_t^{[\ell] (k)} (\psi_t) - F_t^{[\ell] (k)} (\varphi_t) \|_{L^{\infty} (\mathcal{D} ; B)}\\ \qquad \leqslant k \| F \|_{C, \alpha, \beta, \kappa} C^k D^k 2^{(\alpha - \rho) t} 2^{(\rho - \beta) k t} 2^{- \kappa \ell t} \| \psi_t - \varphi_t \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t (\mathcal{D} ; B)}, \end{array}\)

compare with the proof of Lemma 4.22, where we used the triangle inequality to distribute the weight \(\mathrm{e}^{- \xi 2^t \operatorname{dist} (y, B)} \mathrm{e}^{\xi \operatorname{dist} (y, \mathcal{D})}\), for \(\xi\) small enough, on the Steiner tree for any \(j = 1, \ldots, k\):

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{l} \mathrm{e}^{- \xi 2^t \operatorname{dist} (y, B)} \mathrm{e}^{\xi \operatorname{dist} (y, \mathcal{D})} | F_t^{[\ell] (k)} ((y, \mu) ; (x_1, \mu_1) \ldots ; (x_k, \mu_k)) |\\ \qquad \leqslant | F_t^{[\ell] (k)} ((y, \mu) ; (x_1, \mu_1) \ldots ; (x_k, \mu_k)) | w^{(k + 1)}_{t ; c} (y ; x_1 ; \ldots ; x_k) \mathrm{e}^{- \xi 2^t \operatorname{dist} (x_j, B)} \mathrm{e}^{\xi \operatorname{dist} (x_j, \mathcal{D})} . \end{array}\)
\(\Box\)

Proof of Part 2 in Theorem 1.3. By Corollary 4.25 and by duality we have

\(\displaystyle \| \Psi_{\infty} (f^{(j, k)}) \| \lesssim \| f^{(j, k)} \|_{B^{\gamma^+}_{1, 1, 0^-}}, \hspace{3em} \| \Psi_{\infty}^{(i)} (f^{(j, k)}) \| \lesssim \| f^{(j, k)} \|_{B^{\gamma^+}_{1, 1, 0^-}} .\)

Then, by Lemma 4.29 we can write

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{l} \left| \operatorname{Cov}_{\omega^V} \left( \prod_{k = 1}^{m_1} X_{\infty} (f^{(1, k)}) ; \prod_{k = 1}^{m_2} X_{\infty} (f^{(2, k)}) \right) \right|\\ \qquad \lesssim \sum_{i = 1, 2, j = 1, 2, j \neq i} \prod_{k' = 1}^{m_j} \| f^{(j, k')} \|_{B^{\gamma^+}_{1, 1, 0^-}} \left( \sum_k^{m_i} \| (\Psi_{\infty} - \Psi_{\infty}^{(i)}) (f^{(i, k)}) \| \prod_{\ell \neq k} \| f^{(i, \ell)} \|_{B^{\gamma^+}_{1, 1, 0^{}}} \right) \end{array} \) (4.47)

If \(\operatorname{dist} (D_1, D_2) \leqslant 1\), we write \(\| (\Psi_{\infty} - \Psi_{\infty}^{(i)}) (f^{(i, k)}) \| \leqslant \| \Psi_{\infty} (f^{(i, k)}) \| + \| \Psi_{\infty}^{(i)} (f^{(i, k)}) \| \lesssim \| f^{(i, k)} \|_{B^{\gamma^+}_{1, 1, 0^-}}\). Otherwise, we will prove that \(\| (\Psi_{\infty} - \Psi_{\infty}^{(i)}) (f^{(i, k)}) \|\) decays exponentially in \(\operatorname{dist} (\mathcal{D}_i^c, D_i)\), and thus in \(\operatorname{dist} (D_1, D_2)\), since we can choose \(\mathcal{D}_i\) so that \(\min_i \operatorname{dist} (\mathcal{D}_i^c, D_i) \sim \operatorname{dist} (D_1, D_2)\).

First of all, we note that \(\| f \|_{L^1} \lesssim \| f \|_{B^a_{1, 1, 0^-}}\) and hence, since \(\operatorname{supp} (f^{(i, k)}) \subseteq D_i\)

\(\displaystyle \| \mathrm{e}^{- \xi 2^t \operatorname{dist} (\cdot, D_i)} \mathrm{e}^{\xi \operatorname{dist} (\cdot, \mathcal{D}_i^c)} f^{(i, k)} \|_{L^1} \lesssim \| f \|_{B^{\gamma^+}_{1, 1, 0^-}} \mathrm{e}^{- \xi \operatorname{dist} (D_i, \mathcal{D}^c_i)} .\)

Therefore, by Hölder's inequality we obtain

\(\displaystyle \| (\Psi_t - \Psi_t^{(i)}) (f^{(i, k)}) \| \lesssim \| f \|_{B^{\gamma^+}_{1, 1, 0^-}} \mathrm{e}^{- \xi \operatorname{dist} (D_i, \mathcal{D}^c_i)} \| \Psi_t - \Psi_t^{(i)} \|^{}_{L^{\infty}_t (\mathcal{D}^c_i ; D_i)}, \) (4.48)

We first of all control \(\| \Psi_t - \Psi^{(i)}_t \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t (\mathcal{D}^c_i)} \equiv \| \Psi_t - \Psi^{(i)}_t \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t (\mathcal{D}^c_i ; \mathbb{R}^d)}\) and \(\| R_t - R^{(i)}_t \|_{L^{\infty} (\mathcal{D}^c_i)} \equiv \| R_t - R^{(i)}_t \|_{L^{\infty} (\mathcal{D}^c_i ; \mathbb{R}^d)}\). Note that by Lemma 4.30, the following bounds hold for some constant \(\tilde{c}_n\):

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rll} \| \mathrm{D} F_s (\Psi_s) \|_{\mathcal{B}_{\xi} (L^{\infty} ; L^{\infty})} & \leqslant & \tilde{c}_n \lambda 2^{(\alpha - \beta) s}\\ \| F_s (\Psi_s) - F_s (\Psi^{(i)}_s) \|_{L^{\infty} (\mathcal{D}^c_i)} & \leqslant & \tilde{c}_n \lambda 2^{(\alpha - \beta) s} {\| \Psi_s - \Psi^{(i)}_s \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s (\mathcal{D}^c_i)}}_{},\\ \| \mathrm{D} F_s (\Psi_s) - \mathrm{D} F_s (\Psi^{(i)}_s) \|_{\mathcal{B} (L^{\infty} ; L^{\infty} (\mathcal{D}^c_i))} & \leqslant & \tilde{c}_n \lambda 2^{(\alpha - \beta - \gamma) s} {\| \Psi_s - \Psi^{(i)}_s \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s (\mathcal{D}^c_i)}}_{},\\ \| H_{> n ; s} (\Psi_s) - H_{> n ; s} (\Psi^{(i)}_s) \|_{L^{\infty} (\mathcal{D}^c_i)} & \leqslant & \tilde{c}_n \lambda 2^{(\alpha - n \kappa - \gamma) s} {\| \Psi_s - \Psi^{(i)}_s \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s (\mathcal{D}^c_i)}}_{} . \end{array}\)

We plug these bounds in the equations for \(\Psi_t - \Psi^{(i)}_t\) and \(R_t - R^{(i)}_t\), see Lemma 3.22 to obtain, for \(\xi\) small enough

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{l} \| \Psi_t - \Psi^{(i)}_t \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t (\mathcal{D}^c_i)}\\ \quad \leqslant \int_0^t \| \mathrm{e}^{\xi | \cdot |} \dot{G}_s \|_{L^1} \left[^{} \left\| F_s (\Psi_s) - F_s (\Psi^{(i)}_s) \right\|_{L^{\infty} (\mathcal{D}^c_i)}_{} + \left\| R_s - R_s^{(i)} \right\|_{L^{\infty} (\mathcal{D}^c_i)}_{} \right] \mathrm{d} s\\ \hspace{5em} + \| X_t - X_t^{(i)} \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t (\mathcal{D}^c_i)}\\ \quad \lesssim \tilde{c}_n \int_0^t 2^{- (d - 2 \gamma) s} \left[ \lambda 2^{(\alpha - \beta) s} \left\| \Psi_s - \Psi^{(i)}_s \right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s (\mathcal{D}^c_i)}_{} + \left\| R_s - R_s^{(i)} \right\|_{L^{\infty} (\mathcal{D}^c_i)}_{} \right] \mathrm{d} s + 2^{\gamma t}\\ \quad \lesssim \tilde{c}_n \lambda (d - 2 \gamma - \alpha + \beta)^{- 1} 2^{\gamma t} \sup_{s \in \mathbb{R}^+} 2^{- \gamma s} \| \Psi_s - \Psi_s^{(i)} \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s (\mathcal{D}^c_i)}\\ \hspace{6em} + \int_0^{\infty} 2^{- (d - 2 \gamma) s} {\| R_s - R_s^{(i)} \|_{L^{\infty} (\mathcal{D}^c_i)}}_{} \mathrm{d} s + 2^{\gamma t}, \end{array} \) (4.49)

and

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{l} \| R_t - R^{(i)}_t \|_{L^{\infty} (\mathcal{D}^c_i)}\\ \qquad \lesssim \int_t^{\infty} \| H_{> n ; s} (\Psi_s) - H_{> n ; s} (\Psi^{(i)}_s) \|_{L^{\infty} (\mathcal{D}^c_i)} \mathrm{d} s\\ \hspace{4em} + \int_t^{\infty} \| \mathrm{D} F_s (\Psi_s) \|_{\mathcal{B}_{\xi} (L^{\infty} ; L^{\infty})} 2^{- (d - 2 \gamma) s} \| R_s - R^{(i)}_s \|_{L^{\infty} (\mathcal{D}^c_i)} \mathrm{d} s\\ \hspace{4em} + \int_t^{\infty} \| \mathrm{D} F_s (\Psi_s) - \mathrm{D} F_s (\Psi^{(i)}_s) \|_{\mathcal{B} (L^{\infty} ; L^{\infty} (\mathcal{D}^c_i))} 2^{- (d - 2 \gamma) s} {\| R^{(i)}_s \|_{L^{\infty}}}_{} \mathrm{d} s\\ \qquad \lesssim \tilde{c}_n \lambda [1 + c_n \lambda] \sup_{s \in \mathbb{R}^+} 2^{- \gamma s} {\| \Psi_s - \Psi^{(i)}_s \|_{\mathcal{C}^2_s (\mathcal{D}^c_i)}}_{}\\ \hspace{4em} + \lambda \int_t^{\infty} 2^{- (d - 2 \gamma - \alpha + \beta) s} \| R_s - R^{(i)}_s \|_{L^{\infty} (\mathcal{D}^c_i)} \mathrm{d} s \end{array} \) (4.50)

where we used that \(\int_t^{\infty} 2^{- (d - 2 \gamma - \alpha + \beta) s} \| R^{(i)}_s \|_{L^{\infty}} \mathrm{d} s \lesssim c_n \lambda\). For \(\lambda\) small enough, (4.49) and (4.50) imply \(\sup_{t \in \mathbb{R}^+} 2^{- \gamma t} \| \Psi_t - \Psi^{(i)}_t \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t (\mathcal{D}^c_i)} \lesssim 1\) and \(\sup_{t \in \mathbb{R}^+} \| R_t - R^{(i)}_t \|_{L^{\infty} (\mathcal{D}^c_i)} \lesssim_n \lambda\).

We now prove that \(\| \Psi_t - \Psi^{(i)}_t \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t (\mathcal{D}^c_i ; D_i)}\) is actually small, uniformly in \(t \in [0, \infty]\). In fact, by Lemma 4.30, we also have the bound

\(\displaystyle \| F_s (\Psi_s) - F_s (\Psi^{(i)}_s) \|_{L^{\infty} (\mathcal{D}^c_i ; D_i)} \lesssim_n \lambda 2^{(\alpha - \beta) s} {\| \Psi_s - \Psi^{(i)}_s \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s (\mathcal{D}^c_i ; D_i)}}_{},\)

hence

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{l} \| \Psi_t - \Psi^{(i)}_t \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t (\mathcal{D}^c_i ; D_i)}\\ \qquad \lesssim \int_0^t \| \mathrm{e}^{2 \xi | \cdot |} \dot{G}_s \|_{L^1} ^{} \left( \left\| F_s (\Psi_s) - F_s (\Psi^{(i)}_s) \right\|_{L^{\infty}_s (\mathcal{D}^c_i ; D_i)}_{} + \| R_t - R^{(i)}_t \|_{L^{\infty} (\mathcal{D}^c_i)} \right) \mathrm{d} s + 1\\ \qquad \lesssim_n \lambda \int_0^t 2^{- (d - 2 \gamma) s} \left( 2^{(\alpha - \beta) s} \left\| \Psi_s - \Psi^{(i)}_s \right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s (\mathcal{D}^c_i ; D_i)}_{} + 1 \right) \mathrm{d} s + 1. \end{array} \) (4.51)

Since \(d - 2 \gamma - \alpha + \beta > 0\), for \(\lambda\) small enough (4.51) implies uniformly in \(t \in [0, \infty]\)

\(\displaystyle \| \Psi_t - \Psi_t^{(i)} \|^{}_{L^{\infty}_t (\mathcal{D}^c_i ; D_i)} \lesssim 1,\)

which plugged into (4.48) implies the claim, provided that \((\mathcal{D}_i)_i\) are chosen so that \(\min_i \operatorname{dist} (\mathcal{D}_i^c, D_i) \sim \operatorname{dist} (D_1, D_2)\).\(\Box\)

Remark 4.31. It is easy to see that the proof extends trivially to the case of \(n\)-cluster correlation functions. In this case, one finds independent solutions \((\Psi^{(i)})_{i = 1, \ldots, n}\) in each of the clusters, and hence, the exponential clustering follows by simply controlling \(\left\| (\Psi - \Psi^{(i)}) \left( {f^{(i, k)}}^{} \right) \right\|\) precisely as was done above.

Proof of Part 3 in Theorem 1.3. The proof is based on the representation of the two-point function via a generating function. In our setting, a generating function is meaningful provided one introduces some “external” Grassmann variable \(\phi\) compatible with the Grassmann martingale \(X\) (and in particular with \(\psi = X_{\infty}\)) and such that \(\omega (\phi) = \omega_t (\phi) = \phi\). This setting requires a simple modification of the NPS discussed in Section 2, whose details we leave to the reader.

For such a Grassmann variable \(\phi\), and any \(A, B \in \mathcal{A}\) with \(B\) anticommuting with \(\psi\) we have

\(\displaystyle \omega (A \mathrm{e}^{V (\psi) + \phi B}) = \omega (A \mathrm{e}^{V (\psi)}) + \phi \omega (BA \mathrm{e}^{V (\psi)}) .\)

Denote \(\mathcal{G}_{\phi} \cong \mathbb{C} \oplus \phi \mathbb{C}\) and note the identity \((a + b \phi)^{- 1} = a^{- 1} (1 - a^{- 1} b \phi)\) for any \(a \in \mathbb{C} \setminus \{ 0 \}\) and \(b \in \mathbb{C}\). Accordingly, for any \(B \in \mathcal{A}\) anticommuting with \(\psi\) we can define the continuous linear functional on \(\mathcal{A}\)

\(\displaystyle \frac{\omega (\cdot \mathrm{e}^{V (\psi) + \phi B})}{\omega (\mathrm{e}^{V (\psi) + \phi B})} = \omega^V (\cdot) + \phi \operatorname{Cov}_{\omega^V} (B ; \cdot) . \) (4.52)

For the sake of brevity, denote by \(\partial_{\phi} : \mathcal{G}_{\phi} \rightarrow \mathbb{C}\) the projection on the coefficient of \(\phi\). Considering now \(A = \psi (f_1)\) and \(B = \psi (f_2)\), since \(\omega (\psi (f_i)) = 0\), \(i = 1, 2\), we have

\(\displaystyle \omega^V (\psi (f_1) \psi (f_2)) = -\operatorname{Cov}_{\omega^V} (\psi (f_2) ; \psi (f_1)) = - \partial_{\phi} \omega^{\phi, f_2} (\psi (f_1)),\)

where we have introduced the shorthand \(\omega^{\phi, f_2}\) for the l.h.s. of (4.52) with \(B = \psi (f_2)\). As a consequence of Theorem 3.12 the following stochastic quantisation formula holds for \(\omega^{\phi, f}\):

\(\displaystyle \omega^{\phi, f} (A (\psi)) = \omega (A (\Psi_{\infty}^{\phi, f})),\)

where \((\Psi^{\phi, f}_t)_{t \geqslant 0}\) solves the following FBSDE on \([0, \infty]\)

\(\displaystyle \mathrm{d} \Psi^{\phi, f}_t = \phi \dot{G}_t f \mathrm{d} t + \dot{G}_t \omega_t (\mathrm{D} V (\Psi^{\phi, f}_{\infty})) \mathrm{d} t + \mathrm{d} X_t,\)
\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} R^{\phi, f}_t & = & \int_t^{\infty} \omega_t (H_{> n ; s} (\Psi^{\phi, f}_s)) \mathrm{d} s + \int_t^{\infty} \omega_t (\langle \dot{G}_s R^{\phi, f}_s + \phi \dot{G}_t f, \mathrm{D} F_s (\Psi^{\phi, f}_s) \rangle) \mathrm{d} s, \end{array}\)

By simple computations, we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{l} \omega^V (\psi (f_1) \psi (f_2)) - \omega (\psi (f_1) \psi (f_2))\\ \qquad = - \partial_{\phi} \omega (\Psi_{\infty}^{\phi, f_2} (f_1)) - {\langle\!\langle} f_1, Gf_2 {\rangle\!\rangle}_{\Theta} = - \omega (\partial_{\phi} \mathcal{S}_{\infty}^{\phi, f_2} (f_1)), \end{array}\)

where we set

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{S}_t^{\phi, f_2} := \int_0^t \dot{G}_s \omega_s (\mathrm{D} V (\Psi^{\phi, f_2}_{\infty})) \mathrm{d} s.\)

We now write \(\omega_t (\mathrm{D} V (\Psi^{\phi, f}_{\infty})) = F_t (\Psi^{\phi, f}_t) + R_t^{\phi, f}\), where it is crucial to note that \(F\) is independent of \(\phi\) and \(f\), that is, it can be taken as the solution of the truncated Polchinski equation as before. Note in particular that when \(\phi\) is formally set to \(0\) we have that \(\Psi^{\phi = 0, f} = \Psi\), and \(R^{\phi = 0, f} = R\) so that we can write

\(\displaystyle \partial_{\phi} \Psi^{\phi, f} = \Psi^{\phi, f} - \Psi, \hspace{3em} \partial_{\phi} R^{\phi, f} = R^{\phi, f} - R. \) (4.53)

The pair \((\partial_{\phi} \Psi^{\phi, f}, \partial_{\phi} R^{\phi, f})\) solves the forward-backward system

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \partial_{\phi} \Psi^{\phi, f}_t & = & G_t f + \int_0^t \dot{G}_s (\partial_{\phi} F_s (\Psi^{\phi, f}_s) + \partial_{\phi} R^{\phi, f}_s) \mathrm{d} s\\ \partial_{\phi} R^{\phi, f}_t & = & \int_t^{\infty} \omega_t (\partial_{\phi} H_{> n ; s} (\Psi_s^{\phi, f})) \mathrm{d} s + \int_t^{\infty} \omega_t (\mathrm{D} F_s (\Psi_s) \cdot \dot{G}_s \partial_{\phi} R^{\phi, f}_s) \mathrm{d} s\\ & & + \int_t^{\infty} \omega_t (\mathrm{D} F_s (\Psi_s) \cdot \dot{G}_s f) \mathrm{d} s + \int_t^{\infty} \omega_t (\partial_{\phi} \mathrm{D} F_s (\Psi_s^{\phi, f}) \cdot \dot{G}_s R_s) \mathrm{d} s \end{array} \) (4.54)

and we have the representation

\(\displaystyle \partial_{\phi} \mathcal{S}_t^{\phi, f_2} = \int_0^t \dot{G}_s (\partial_{\phi} F_s (\Psi_s^{\phi, f_2}) + \partial_{\phi} R^{\phi, f_2}_s) \mathrm{d} s. \) (4.55)

Along the lines of the proof of Proposition 4.20, we can show that the system (4.54) with \(f\) such that \(\| f \|_{L^1} = 1\) has a unique global solution \((\partial_{\phi} \Psi^{\phi, f}, \partial_{\phi} R^{\phi, f})\) satisfying

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980} (\partial_{\phi} \Psi^{\phi, f}, \partial_{\phi} R^{\phi, f}) \unicode{x2980}_{\ast} \lesssim 1, \) (4.56)

with \(\sigma = 2 \gamma\), \(\sigma' = 0\), \(a = D\), \(a' = c_n \lambda\) and \(\eta = 0\), these constants appearing in the definition of the norm (4.28). First of all, noting that \(\partial_{\phi} F_s (\Psi^{\phi, f}_s) = F_s (\Psi^{\phi, f}_s) - F_s (\Psi_s)\) and so on, and taking (4.53) into account, the following estimates are a consequence of Lemma 4.22

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rll} \| \partial_{\phi} F_s (\Psi^{\phi, f}_s) \|_{L^{\infty}} & \lesssim & \lambda 2^{(\alpha - \beta) s} \| \partial_{\phi} \Psi_s^{\phi, f_2} \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s},\\ \| \partial_{\phi} H_{> n ; s} (\Psi_s^{\phi, f}) \|_{L^{\infty}} & \lesssim & \lambda 2^{(\alpha - n \kappa - \gamma) s} \| \partial_{\phi} \Psi_s^{\phi, f_2} \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s},\\ \| \partial_{\phi} \mathrm{D} F_s (\Psi_s^{\phi, f}) \|_{L^{\infty}} & \lesssim & \lambda 2^{(\alpha - \beta - \gamma) s} \| \partial_{\phi} \Psi_s^{\phi, f_2} \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s} . \end{array}\)

Then, by Young's inequality for convolutions we have the bound \(\| G_t f \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t} {\lesssim 2^{2 \gamma t}}^{}\) whereas the drift is estimated as

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{l} \| \dot{G}_s (\partial_{\phi} F_s (\Psi_s) + \partial_{\phi} R^{\phi, f}_s) \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s}\\ \qquad \lesssim 2^{- (d - 2 \gamma) s} (\lambda 2^{(\alpha - \beta) s} \| \partial_{\phi} \Psi_s^{\phi, f_2} \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s} + \| \partial_{\phi} R^{\phi, f}_s \|_{L^{\infty}}) . \end{array}\)

On the other hand, the terms in the equation for \(\partial_{\phi} R^{\phi, f}_t\) are bounded as follows

\(\displaystyle \| \omega_t (\partial_{\phi} H_{> n ; s} (\Psi_s^{\phi, f})) \|_{L^{\infty}} \lesssim \lambda 2^{(\alpha - n \kappa - \gamma) s} \| \partial_{\phi} \Psi_s^{\phi, f} \|_{L^{\infty}},\)
\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| \omega_t (\mathrm{D} F_s (\Psi_s) \cdot \dot{G}_s \partial_{\phi} R^{\phi, f}_s) \|_{L^{\infty}} & \lesssim & \| \mathrm{D} F_s (\Psi_s) \|_{\mathcal{B} (L^{\infty} ; L^{\infty})} \| \dot{G}_s \|_{L^1} \| \partial_{\phi} R^{\phi, f}_s \|_{L^{\infty}}\\ & \lesssim & 2^{- (d - 2 \gamma - \alpha + \beta) s} \| \partial_{\phi} R^{\phi, f}_s \|_{L^{\infty}}, \end{array}\)
\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| \omega_t (\mathrm{D} F_s (\Psi_s) \cdot \dot{G}_s f) \|_{L^{\infty}} & \lesssim & \| \mathrm{D} F_s (\Psi_s) \|_{\mathcal{B} (L^{\infty} ; L^{\infty})} \| \dot{G}_s \|_{L^1} \| f \|_{L^1}\\ & \lesssim & 2^{- (d - 2 \gamma - \alpha + \beta) s}, \end{array}\)
\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| \omega_t (\partial_{\phi} \mathrm{D} F_s (\Psi_s^{\phi, f}) \cdot \dot{G}_s R_s) \|_{L^{\infty}} & \lesssim & \| \partial_{\phi} \mathrm{D} F_s (\Psi_s^{\phi, f}) \|_{L^{\infty}} \| \dot{G}_s \|_{L^1} \| R_s \|_{L^{\infty}}\\ & \lesssim & 2^{- (d - \gamma - \alpha + \beta)} \| \partial_{\phi} \Psi_s^{\phi, f_2} \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s} . \end{array}\)

From these bounds, following exactly the same steps carried out in the proof of Proposition 4.20 we have that the system (4.54) has a unique solution in the ball defined in (4.56).

We will henceforth fix \(\varepsilon > 0\) and take \(f_i := \chi^{\varepsilon}_{x_i}\) where \(\chi^{\varepsilon}_x \in C^{\infty}_c\) is as in the statement of the theorem, so that \(\| f_i \|_{L^1} = 1\). Set \(B_i := \{ x \in \mathbb{R}^d | | x - x_i | \leqslant \varepsilon \}\), \(i = 1, 2\). For \(\xi\) small enough, using \(\| f_2 \|_{L^1} = 1\) and the estimates in Corollary 3.24 we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| G_t f_2 \|_{\mathcal{C}^n_t (\mathbb{R}^d ; B_1)} & \leqslant & \int_0^t \| \dot{G}_s f_2 \|_{\mathcal{C}^n_s (\mathbb{R}^d ; B_1)} \mathrm{d} s\\ & \leqslant & \int_0^t \sup_{\nu} 2^{- ns} \| \partial^n_{\nu} \dot{G}_s \|_{L_{s ; \xi}^{\infty}} 2^{- \xi 2^s \operatorname{dist} (B_2, B_1)} \| f_2 \|_{L^1}\\ & \lesssim & \int_0^t 2^{- \xi 2^s \operatorname{dist} (B_1, B_2)} 2^{2 \gamma s} \mathrm{d} s\\ & \lesssim & \operatorname{dist} (B_1, B_2)^{- 2 \gamma} . \end{array}\)

where the norm \(\| \cdot \|_{\mathcal{C}^n_t (\mathcal{D} ; B)}\) for measurable sets \(\mathcal{D}, B \subseteq \mathbb{R}^d\) was defined in (4.44). Furthermore, by the triangle inequality

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{l} \int_0^t \| \dot{G}_s \partial_{\phi} F_s (\Psi_s^{\phi, f_2}) \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant n}_s (\mathbb{R}^d ; B_1)} \mathrm{d} s\\ \quad \lesssim \int_0^t \sup_{\nu} 2^{- ns} \| \dot{G}_s \|_{L^1_{\xi, 1, s}} \| \partial_{\phi} \Psi_s^{\phi, f_2} \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s (\mathbb{R}^d ; B_1)} \| \mathrm{D} F_s (\Psi_s) \|_{\mathcal{B}_s (L^{\infty} ; L^{\infty})} \mathrm{d} s\\ \quad \lesssim \lambda \int_0^t 2^{- (d - 2 \gamma - \alpha + \beta) s} \| \partial_{\phi} \Psi_s^{\phi, f_2} \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s (\mathbb{R}^d ; B_1)} \mathrm{d} s\\ \quad \lesssim \lambda \sup_{s \in \mathbb{R}^+} \| \partial_{\phi} \Psi_s^{\phi, f_2} \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_s (\mathbb{R}^d ; B_1)} . \end{array}\)

Furthermore,

\(\displaystyle \int_0^t \| \dot{G}_s \partial_{\phi} R^{\phi, f_2}_s \|_{L^{\infty}_t (\mathbb{R}^d ; B_1)} \mathrm{d} s \leqslant \int_0^t \| \dot{G}_s \|_{L^1_{s ; \xi}} \| \partial_{\phi} R^{\phi, f_2}_s \|_{L^{\infty}} \mathrm{d} s \lesssim \lambda,\)

hence

\(\displaystyle \sup_{t \in \mathbb{R}^+} \| \partial_{\phi} \Psi_t^{\phi, f_2} \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t (\mathbb{R}^d ; B_1)} \lesssim \lambda +\operatorname{dist} (B_1, B_2)^{- 2 \gamma} + \lambda \sup_{t \in \mathbb{R}^+} \| \partial_{\phi} \Psi_t^{\phi, f_2} \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t (\mathbb{R}^d ; B_1)}\)

implying, for \(\operatorname{dist} (B_1, B_2)\) small enough

\(\displaystyle \sup_{t \in \mathbb{R}^+} \| \partial_{\phi} \Psi_t^{\phi, f_2} \|_{\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 2}_t (\mathbb{R}^d ; B_1)} \lesssim \operatorname{dist} (B_1, B_2)^{- 2 \gamma} .\)

We plug this bound into the equation for \(\partial_{\phi} \mathcal{S}_t^{\phi, f_2} (f_1)\), see (4.55), and obtain for \(\xi\) small enough

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| \partial_{\phi} \mathcal{S}_t^{\phi, f_2} \|_{L^{\infty}_t (\mathbb{R}^d ; B_1)} & \lesssim & \int_0^t 2^{- (d - 2 \gamma) s} (\operatorname{dist} (B_1, B_2)^{- 2 \gamma} \lambda 2^{(\alpha - \beta) s} + \lambda) \mathrm{d} s\\ & \lesssim & \lambda \operatorname{dist} (B_1, B_2)^{- 2 \gamma}, \end{array}\)

where we used once more that \(d - 2 \gamma - \alpha + \beta > 0\). Since \(f_1\) is supported on \(B_1\), we have \(\| \partial_{\phi} \mathcal{S}_t^{\phi, f_2} (f_1) \|_{L^{\infty}_t (\mathbb{R}^d ; B_1)} \lesssim \| \partial_{\phi} \mathcal{S}_t^{\phi, f_2} \|_{L^{\infty}_t (\mathbb{R}^d ; B_1)} \| f_1 \|_{L^1}\), hence the claim.\(\Box\)

Appendix AProof of Theorem 2.20

In this section we prove the existence of GBM as described in Theorem 2.20 and in Definition 3.18. More precisely, we prove the existence of a NPS where our GBMs are realised as suitable operators acting on a Hilbert space. The construction of Grassmann fields as operators acting on a Hilbert space was already obtained in the early days of Euclidean quantum field theory [OS72, OS73, Ost73], see also [GJ87]. More recently, this algebraic approach has been further developed in [ABDG20] with applications to Grassmann SDE. In addition to what was already done there, here we construct a conditional expectation and discuss GBMs in the framework of FNPST.

The proof of Theorem 2.20 is by construction and divided into three steps. First of all, we associate a FNPS with the pair \((\mathcal{H}, (P_t)_{t \geqslant 0})\) consisting of a Hilbert space \(\mathcal{H}\), which in our case will be chosen to be \(L^2 (\mathbb{R}_+ ; \mathfrak{h})\), and a net of projections \((P_t)_{t \geqslant 0}\). This association will be called Fock construction for obvious reasons. Then, we construct a conditional expectation associated with the Fock construction. Finally, we provide an explicit representation of the GBM by using the Osterwalder–Schrader construction [OS72, OS73, Ost73].

Definition A.1. (Fock construction). Let \(\mathcal{H}\) be a separable Hilbert space and \((P_t)_{t \geqslant 0}\) an increasing orthogonal net of projections on it, that is, for any \(s \leqslant t\) we have

\(\displaystyle P_t P_s = P_{t \wedge s} = P_s P_t .\) (A.1)

The Fock construction associated with the pair \((\mathcal{H}, (P_t)_{t \geqslant 0})\) is the FNPS \((\mathcal{M}, \omega, (\mathcal{M}_t)_{t \geqslant 0})\), where \(\omega (\cdot) := \left\langle \Omega, \cdot \, \Omega \right\rangle\) with \(\Omega\) being the Fock vacuum on \(\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H})\), the antisymmetric Fock space generated by \(\mathcal{H}\), and where

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{M} := \operatorname{CAR} (\mathcal{H}), \qquad \mathcal{M}_t := \operatorname{CAR} (P_t \mathcal{H}) \subseteq \mathcal{M}.\)

Remark A.2. Verifying that the triple \((\mathcal{M}, \omega, (\mathcal{M}_t)_{t \geqslant 0})\) is a FNPS is a simple check, see the definitions in Section 2.

Although the Fock construction is very simple, there is no general theory of conditional expectations, because the vector \(\Omega\), does not have the modular property [Tom67, Tak70, Tak72]. If this were the case, on the other hand, the existence of a conditional expectation of \(\mathcal{M}\) onto \(\mathcal{M}_t\) would be a consequence of a theorem by Takesaki [Tak72]. Nonetheless, a conditional expectation can be explicitly defined by means of the fermionic exponential law [Der06].

For any \(s \leqslant t\), we write

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{H}_t := P_t \mathcal{H}, \quad \mathcal{H}_{s, t} := (\mathbb{1} - P_s) P_t \mathcal{H} = (\mathbb{1} - P_s) \mathcal{H}_t, \quad \mathcal{H}_{> t} := (\mathbb{1} - P_t) \mathcal{H} .\)

By the property (A.1), we have that for any \(s \leqslant t\) the subspaces \(\mathcal{H}_s\), \(\mathcal{H}_{s, t}\) and \(\mathcal{H}_{> t}\) are orthogonal and we get \(\mathcal{H} = \mathcal{H}_s \oplus \mathcal{H}_{s, t} \oplus \mathcal{H}_{> t} = \mathcal{H}_t \oplus \mathcal{H}_{> t}\).

The exponential law for fermions is the unitary operator \(U_t : \Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_t \oplus \mathcal{H}_{> t}) \rightarrow \Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_t) \otimes \Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_{> t})\), such that

\(\displaystyle U_t a (f) U_t^{\ast} = a (P_t f) \otimes \mathbb{1} + \Xi_t \otimes a ((\mathbb{1} - P_t) f) \;, \hspace{4em} U_t \Omega = \Omega_t \otimes \Omega_{> t} \;, \) (A.2)

where \(\Xi_t = (- 1)^{N_t}\)

A.1. Note that \(\Xi_t\) is sometimes called parity operator of \(\mathcal{H}_t\).

A.1 , where \(N_t\) is the number operator on \(\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_t)\), and \(\Omega, \Omega_t, \Omega_{> t}\) are the ground states of \(\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H})\), \(\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_t)\) and \(\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_{> t})\) respectively. Furthermore, for any \(s < t\), we can define the unitary operator \(U_{s, t} : \Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_s \oplus \mathcal{H}_{s, t} \oplus \mathcal{H}_{> t}) \rightarrow \Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_s) \otimes \Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_{s, t}) \otimes \Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_{> t})\) such that

\(\displaystyle U_{s, t} a (f) U_{s, t}^{\ast} = a (P_s f) \otimes \mathbb{1} \otimes \mathbb{1} + \Xi_s \otimes a (P_{s, t} f) \otimes \mathbb{1} + \Xi_s \otimes \Xi_{s, t} \otimes a ((\mathbb{1} - P_t) f)\) (A.3)

where \(P_{s, t} = P_t (\mathbb{1} - P_s) = (\mathbb{1} - P_s) P_t = P_t - P_s\), and

\(\displaystyle U_{s, t} \Omega = \Omega_s \otimes \Omega_{s, t} \otimes \Omega_{> t}\)

where \(\Omega_{s, t}\) is the ground state of \(\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_{s, t})\), and \(\Xi_{s, t} = (- 1)^{N_{s, t}}\) where \(N_{s, t}\) is the number operator of \(\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_{s, t})\). It is important to note the following relation between \(U_{s, t}\), \(U_s\) and \(U_t\), namely, for any \(D \in \operatorname{CAR} (\mathcal{H})\) we have

\(\displaystyle U_{s, t} D U_{s, t}^{\ast} = (U_s \otimes \mathbb{1}) (U_t D U_t^{\ast}) (U_s^{\ast} \otimes \mathbb{1}) = (\mathbb{1} \otimes U_t) (U_s D U_s^{\ast}) (\mathbb{1} \otimes U_t^{\ast}) . \) (A.4)

Remark A.3. Hereafter if \(\mathcal{K} \subset \mathcal{H}\) we identify \(\operatorname{CAR} (\mathcal{K})\) with a subspace of \(\operatorname{CAR} (\mathcal{H})\) in the following way

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{l} \operatorname{CAR} (\mathcal{K})\\ \qquad =\operatorname{span} \{ a (f_1) \cdots a (f_n) a^{\ast} (g_1) \cdots a^{\ast} (g_m) |n, m \in \mathbb{N}, (f_i)_{= 1}^n, (g_i)_{= 1}^m \subset \mathcal{K}, \} \end{array} . \) (A.5)

In this way if \(A \in \mathcal{B} (\Gamma_a (\mathcal{K})) \cap \operatorname{CAR} (\mathcal{K})\)we can define \(\omega (A)\) as \(\omega (A) = \langle \Omega, A \Omega \rangle\), where \(\Omega\) is the ground state of \(\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H})\) and \(A\) is identified as an operator of \(\operatorname{CAR} (\mathcal{H}) \subset \mathcal{B} (\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}))\) thanks to the relation (A.5). With the previous notation, for any \(s < t\) and \(A \in \operatorname{CAR} (\mathcal{H}_s)\), \(B \in \operatorname{CAR} (\mathcal{H}_{s, t})\) and \(C \in \operatorname{CAR} (\mathcal{H}_{> t})\), we have

\(\displaystyle \omega (A) = \langle \Omega_s, A \Omega_s \rangle, \quad \omega (B) = \langle \Omega_{s, t}, B \Omega_{s, t} \rangle, \quad \quad \omega (C) = \langle \Omega_{> t}, C \Omega_{> t} \rangle .\)

Using the fact that \(U_t\) is an isometry which sends the ground state of \(\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H})\) into the one of \(\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_t) \otimes \Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_{> t})\), and using the previously described notation we get the equality

\(\displaystyle \omega \left( U_t^{\ast} \left( \sum^n_{i = 1} A_i \otimes B_i \right) U_t \right) = \sum^n_{i = 1} \omega (A_i) \omega (B_i) \) (A.6)

for any \(t \in \mathbb{R}_+\), let \(A_i \in \operatorname{CAR} (\mathcal{H}_t)\) and \(B_i \in \operatorname{CAR} (\mathcal{H}_{> t})\).

The exponential law gives another representation of the CAR algebra we consider, as a \(\ast\)-sub-algebra of the algebra of bounded operators on \(\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_t) \otimes \Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_{> t})\). On the tensor product \(\mathcal{B} (\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_t)) \otimes \mathcal{B} (\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_{> t}))\), we introduce the projection map \(\tilde{\Omega}_{> t} : \mathcal{B} (\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_t)) \otimes \mathcal{B} (\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_{> t})) \rightarrow \mathcal{B} (\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_t)) \otimes \mathcal{B} (\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_{> t}))\):

\(\displaystyle \tilde{\Omega}_{> t} (A \otimes B) := A \otimes \mathbb{1} \langle \Omega_{> t}, B \Omega_{> t} \rangle .\)

The following properties hold true.

Lemma A.4. The map \(\tilde{\Omega}_{> t}\) is positive and continuous in the operator norm.

Proof. Consider a generic element \(M = \sum_j A_j \otimes B_j \in \mathcal{B} (\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_t)) \otimes \mathcal{B} (\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_{> t}))\), the sum being finite. Set \(\tilde{A}_j := A_j \langle \Omega_{> t}, B_j \Omega_{> t} \rangle\) and \(A := \sum_j \tilde{A}_j\). To prove positivity, we note that an operator of the form \(A \otimes \mathbb{1}\) is positive provided that \(A\) is positive. We have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \langle \psi, A \psi \rangle_{\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_t)} & = & \sum_j \langle \psi, A_j \psi \rangle_{\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_t)} \langle \Omega_{> t}, B_j \Omega_{> t} \rangle_{\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_{> t})}\\ & = & \langle \psi \otimes \Omega_{> t}, M \psi \otimes \Omega_{> t} \rangle_{\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_t) \otimes \Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_{> t})} \geqslant 0. \end{array}\)

To prove continuity, we note that \(\| A \otimes \mathbb{1} \|_{\mathcal{B} (\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_t) \otimes \Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_{> t}))} \leqslant \| A \|_{\mathcal{B} (\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_t))}\), hence we have the bound:

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{l} \| \tilde{\Omega}_{> t} (M) \|_{\mathcal{B} (\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_t) \otimes \Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_{> t}))} = \| A \otimes \mathbb{1} \|_{\mathcal{B} (\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_t) \otimes \Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_{> t}))}\\ \qquad \leqslant \| A \|_{\mathcal{B} (\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_t))} = \sup_{\psi : \| \psi \|_{\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_t)} = 1} \left| \sum_j \langle \psi, A_j \psi \rangle \langle \Omega_{> t}, B_j \Omega_{> t} \rangle \right|\\ \qquad \leqslant \| M \|_{\mathcal{B} (\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_t) \otimes \Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_{> t}))} . \end{array}\)
\(\Box\)

As a consequence of Lemma A.4, we can extend \(\tilde{\Omega}_{> t}\) to the whole CAR algebra of \(\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_t) \otimes \Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_{> t})\). We finally define the conditional expectation on \(\mathcal{M}_t\).

Definition A.5. (Conditional Expectation). Let \((\Omega, \mathcal{M}, (\mathcal{M}_t)_{t \geqslant 0})\) be the Fock construction associated with \((\mathcal{H}, (P_t)_{t \geqslant 0})\). Let \(U_t\) be the fermionic exponential law and \(\tilde{\Omega}_{> t}\) be the (extension of the) projection defined above. We define the map \(\omega_t : \mathcal{M} \rightarrow \mathcal{M}_t\) by:

\(\displaystyle \omega_t (\cdot) := U_t^{\ast} \tilde{\Omega}_{> t} (U_t \cdot U_t^{\ast}) U_t .\)

Using the notation of Remark A.3, for any \(A_i \in \operatorname{CAR} (\mathcal{H}_t)\) and \(B_i \in \operatorname{CAR} (\mathcal{H}_{> t})\) we have

\(\displaystyle \omega_t \left( U_t^{\ast} \left( \sum_{i = 1}^n A_i \otimes B_i \right) U_t \right) = U_t^{\ast} \tilde{\Omega}_{> t} \left( \sum_{i = 1}^n A_i \otimes B_i \right) U_t = \sum_{i = 1}^n \omega (B_i) A_i . \) (A.7)

Proposition A.6. The map \(\omega_t : \mathcal{M} \rightarrow \mathcal{M}_t\) defined above is a conditional expectation.

Proof. The property \(\omega_t \left( {\mathbb{1}_{\mathcal{M}}}_{} \right) = \mathbb{1}_{\mathcal{M}_t}\) is a straightforward consequence of the definition. The positivity of \(\omega_t (\cdot)\) follows because unitary conjugation preserves positivity and because the map \(\tilde{\Omega}_{> t}\) is positive, see Lemma A.4. We are left with checking the property

\(\displaystyle \omega_t (N_1 M N_2) = N_1 \omega_t (M) N_2,\)

for any \(M \in \mathcal{M}\) and \(N_1, N_2 \in \mathcal{M}_t\). To this end, it suffices to note that operators such as \(N_1\) and \(N_2\) are mapped to operators of the form \(U_t N_i U^{\ast}_t = \tilde{N}_i \otimes \mathbb{1}\), for some \(\tilde{N}_i \in \mathcal{B} (\Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_t))\). Therefore, if \(U_t M U^{\ast}_t = \sum_j A_j \otimes B_j\), the sum being finite, we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \omega_t (N_1 M N_2) & = & U_t^{\ast} \tilde{\Omega}_{> t} (U_t N_1 M N_2 U^{\ast}_t) U_t\\ & = & U_t^{\ast} (\tilde{N}_1 \otimes \mathbb{1})^{} \sum_j (A_j \langle \Omega_{> t}, B_j \Omega_{> t} \rangle \otimes \mathbb{1}) (\tilde{N}_2 \otimes \mathbb{1}) U_t\\ & = & N_1 \omega_t (M) N_2 . \end{array}\)

By the continuity of \(\omega_t (\cdot)\) this extends to any \(M \in \mathcal{M}\).

The fact that \(\omega (\omega_t (D)) = \omega (D)\) for any \(D \in \operatorname{CAR} (\mathcal{H})\), follows from the continuity of \(\omega_t\) and from (A.6). We have now to prove the tower property, i.e. for any \(D \in \operatorname{CAR} (\mathcal{H})\) we have \(\omega_s (\omega_t (K)) = \omega_s (K)\). By the continuity of \(\omega_s, \omega_t\) it is enough to prove the tower property for \(D\) of the form

\(\displaystyle D = U_{s, t}^{\ast} \left( \sum_{i = 1}^n A_i \otimes B_i \otimes C_i \right) U_{s, t}\)

for some \(n \in \mathbb{N}\) and \(A_i \in \operatorname{CAR} (\mathcal{H}_s)\), \(B_i \in \operatorname{CAR} (\mathcal{H}_{s, t})\) and \(C_i \in \Gamma_a (\mathcal{H}_{> t})\). By (A.4) and (A.7) we have

\(\displaystyle \omega_t \left( U_{s, t}^{\ast} \left( \sum_{i = 1}^n A_i \otimes B_i \otimes C_i \right) U_{s, t} \right)\) \(\displaystyle =\) \(\displaystyle \omega_t \left( U_t^{\ast} \left( \sum_{i = 1}^n (U_s^{\ast} (A_i \otimes B_i) U_s) \otimes C_i \right) U_t \right)\)
\(\displaystyle \) \(\displaystyle =\) \(\displaystyle \sum_{i = 1}^n \omega (C_i) U_s^{\ast} (A_i \otimes B_i) U_s .\) (A.8)

Thus, again by (A.4), (A.7) and (A.8), we get

\begin{eqnarray*} \omega_s \left( \omega_t \left( \sum_{i = 1}^n U_{s, t}^{\ast} (A_i \otimes B_i \otimes C_i) U_{s, t} \right) \right) & = & \omega_s \left( \sum_{i = 1}^n \omega (C_i) U_s^{\ast} (A_i \otimes B_i) U_s \right)\\ & = & \sum_{i = 1}^n \omega (C_i) \omega_s (U_s^{\ast} (A_i \otimes B_i) U_s)\\ & = & \sum_{i = 1}^n A_i \omega (C_i) \omega (B_i)\\ & = & \omega_s \left( \sum_{i = 1}^n U_s^{\ast} [A_i \otimes \{ U_t^{\ast} (B_i \otimes C_i) U_t \}] U_s \right)\\ & = & \omega_s \left( U_{s, t}^{\ast} \left( \sum_{i = 1}^n A_i \otimes B_i \otimes C_i \right) U_{s, t} \right) . \end{eqnarray*}

\(\Box\)

Before providing the construction of the GBM, let us set some further notation.
